				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/test_snapshot.js'>test_snapshot.js</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>test_snapshot.js</code> file is a test suite for the Playwright library to ensure that the dashboard renders correctly on localhost at port 5173<br>- It logs various events and checks if the root element has content before taking a screenshot<br>- The code uses <code>chromium.launch()</code> to start a browser, navigate to the specified URL, wait for the page to load, check for React rendering, count the number of children in the root element, and take a screenshot if necessary.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/README_Generator_Colab.ipynb'>README_Generator_Colab.ipynb</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Upload your project folder to Google Drive-Click on the File" menu in the top left corner of the Colab interface.-Select Upload files.-Choose your project folder and click Open.2<br>- <strong>Mount Google Drive (Cell 1)-In the notebook, you can use the following code to mount your Google Drive: `<code><code>python from google.colab import drive # Mount the Google Drive drive.mount(/content/drive) </code></code><code>3<br>- </strong>Set your project path (Cell 2)-After mounting, you can set the project path using the following code: </code>`<code>python project_path = /content/drive/MyDrive/YourProject </code>``4<br>- **Run all cells!-Click on the Run button in the top right corner of the Colab interface to execute all the cells.This setup allows you to quickly generate a README.md and README.html for your project using Google Colabs free AI models, without needing to install any additional libraries or APIs.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/verify_extension.js'>verify_extension.js</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- False` option is used to run the browser without displaying any UI, which is useful for testing purposes.The script also includes error handling to catch and log any errors that occur during the execution of the script<br>- If the headless: false fails (no display), it falls back to a sanity check by checking if the extension path exists in the current directory<br>- This ensures that the script can run without requiring an active browser session.Overall, this script is useful for testing and development purposes, as it allows you to verify that a specific extension is installed and navigates to a specific page without needing a real browser session.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/colab_readme_generator.py'>colab_readme_generator.py</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The script generates a README file that includes information about the project, its purpose, and how to use it<br>- It also includes links to relevant resources and documentation.### Use of the Code File:1<br>- <strong>Upload the ScriptCopy the contents of <code>colab_readme_generator.py</code> into your Google Colab notebook or directly upload it.2<br>- </strong>Run the ScriptExecute the script using the command: <code>!python colab_readme_generator.py--project-path /path/to/project</code>.3<br>- <strong>Accessing ResourcesThe script provides links to relevant resources and documentation, such as tutorials, API documentation, and community forums.### Additional Data:-</strong>Project StructureThe project is structured with a directory structure similar to the one provided in the context details.-<strong>File PathThe file path is specified in the <code>--project-path</code> argument.-</strong>AI ModelThe AI model used is gemini-2.5-flash-lite, which is fast and reliable for trading.This script provides a basic framework for generating README files, but it can be expanded with additional features such as integrating with other tools or services to enhance the projects functionality.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/README_NEW.html'>README_NEW.html</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The code file <code>TradingView Recreation-Market Workstation Platform</code> serves as a platform for trading and market research<br>- It provides a user-friendly interface for users to interact with the platform, including creating, viewing, and analyzing historical data on various financial instruments<br>- The platform uses a combination of web technologies, including HTML, CSS, and JavaScript, to create an interactive and responsive user experience.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/test_n8n_from_container.sh'>test_n8n_from_container.sh</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- All API Endpoints ReachableThe script checks if all necessary endpoints are reachable from the n8n container.-<strong>Autopilot Run Executed SuccessfullyIt triggers an autopilot run to simulate workflow execution.-</strong>Network Connectivity VerifiedIt verifies that the network connection is stable.-<strong>Workflow Will Work Tomorrow at 9:30 AMIt simulates a workflow execution at 9:30 AM, which should trigger the autopilot run.## Additional Instructions:1<br>- </strong>Avoid Using Words Like This file, The file, This code, etc.<strong>2<br>- </strong>Do not include quotes, code snippets, bullets, or lists in your response.<strong>3<br>- </strong>RESPONSE LENGTH: 50-70 words.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/Makefile'>Makefile</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Flask ApplicationProvides a basic web server using Flask.-<strong>Backend APIOffers endpoints for interacting with the Flask application.-</strong>TestingIncludes unit tests for both the backend and frontend.### Use of Code File:The code is structured as a Makefile, which automates the build process<br>- The <code>install</code> task installs all necessary dependencies from a specified directory (<code>phase1/requirements.txt</code>)<br>- The <code>run</code> task runs the Flask application using <code>npm install</code>, followed by running the test scripts in the <code>frontend</code> directory.### Additional Data:-<strong>Project StructureThe project is structured as a single directory with subdirectories for frontend and backend.-</strong>DependenciesThe project uses pip to install dependencies from <code>phase1/requirements.txt</code>.-**Testing ScriptsThe project includes unit tests for both the backend and frontend using pytest, which are located in the <code>frontend</code> directory.This setup allows for easy deployment and maintenance of the application, making it easier to manage dependencies and perform testing.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/generate_readme_html.py'>generate_readme_html.py</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This script generates a professional HTML README file from a Markdown file<br>- The README is designed to be visually appealing and easy to navigate, with clear headings, images, and links<br>- It also includes badges (shields.io style) for Python, TypeScript, React, and FastAPI<br>- The README is generated in the same directory as the Markdown file and is saved as an HTML file with a <code>.html</code> extension.To use this script, save it to a file named <code>generate_readme_html.py</code>, make it executable with <code>chmod +x generate_readme_html.py</code>, and run it from the command line with the path to your Markdown file as an argument<br>- The generated HTML file will be saved in the same directory as the Markdown file.This README is designed to be easy to read, visually appealing, and navigate, making it a valuable resource for anyone working on open-source projects.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/run_all.sh'>run_all.sh</a></b></td>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Playwright SetupIt initializes a new browser instance with <code>headless: true</code> to run in headless mode, which is useful for running tests without opening a graphical user interface.2<br>- <strong>Context CreationA new context is created using <code>browser.newContext()</code>, which allows you to create multiple pages within the same browser session.3<br>- </strong>Page InitializationA new page is created using <code>context.newPage()</code>, which represents a single web page on the platform.4<br>- <strong>Console LoggingThe script logs messages to the console, including error messages, to help identify and resolve issues.5<br>- </strong>Screenshot GenerationThe script takes a screenshot of the current page using <code>page.screenshot({ path: screenshotPath, fullPage: true })</code>, which saves the image to a specified path.6<br>- <strong>Error HandlingIf any errors occur during the execution of the script, they are logged to the console and an error message is displayed.7<br>- </strong>CleanupThe script ensures that the browser is closed after the test completes using <code>browser.close()</code>.8<br>- **Log File CreationThe script writes log messages to a file named <code>app_logs/browser_console.log</code>, which can be used for debugging purposes.This script provides a basic framework for automating gate verification tasks on a tradingview platform, making it easier to integrate into larger projects or scripts that require similar functionality.</td>

							<td style='padding: 8px;'>PurposeAutomates the generation of an AG report based on a provided plan.-<strong>Use CaseUsed for generating reports from automated testing environments or for automating the process of running tests.-</strong>DependenciesRequires <code>playwright</code>, <code>fs</code>, <code>path</code>, and <code>plan.json</code> files.-<strong>Execution Steps-Reads the plan from <code>PLAN_PATH</code>.-Launches a browser using <code>chromium.launch({ headless:!isHeaded })</code>.-Creates a new context and navigates to specified URLs.-Executes each step in the plan, logging results to <code>RESULTS_PATH</code> and saving artifacts to <code>ARTIFACTS_DIR</code>.-</strong>OutputThe script writes the results to <code>SUMMARY_PATH</code>, indicating whether all steps passed or failed.This script is useful for automating the process of running tests and generating reports, making it easier to manage and maintain.</td>
						</tr>
						<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
							<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/scripts/run_3loop_frontend.ts'>run_3loop_frontend.ts</a></b></td>
							<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>run_3loop_frontend.ts</code> file is a script that runs multiple test loops on a frontend application using Node.js and the <code>child_process</code> module to execute shell commands<br>- The script defines an array of loop objects, each with a name and command to run<br>- It then iterates over these loops, executing each one in parallel using <code>execSync</code>, logging the result of each loop<br>- If all loops pass, it logs a success message; otherwise, it logs a failure message and exits with a non-zero status code.This script is useful for automating testing and ensuring that different parts of the application are functioning correctly.</td>
						</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>Toast</code> component is a utility function that manages a list of toast notifications<br>- It provides methods to add, remove, and display these notifications<br>- The component uses Reacts context API to manage the state of the toast list, ensuring that each notification has a unique ID and can be easily removed or displayed.The <code>useToast</code> hook is used to access the toast list and its methods from outside the component<br>- The <code>ToastProvider</code> is responsible for managing the toast list and providing the necessary UI components.The <code>ToastItem</code> component is a simple wrapper around the toast data, including the icon, message, action label, and a close button<br>- It also includes a click event handler to remove the toast when clicked.Overall, the <code>Toast</code> component provides a robust way to manage and display notifications in an open-source project, ensuring that users can easily access and interact with their applications UI.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>ChartEngine</code> class is designed to render a candlestick chart on an HTML canvas<br>- It takes in a canvas element and options to configure the charts size, width, height, and default visible count<br>- The class provides methods to set data, start and stop the chart, and render the chart based on the provided data.The <code>resize</code> method adjusts the canvas dimensions based on the new window size or the specified width and height<br>- It also updates the bar width and calculates the visible range for rendering.The <code>setData</code> method allows updating the chart's data with a new array of candlestick objects.The <code>start</code> and <code>stop</code> methods enable and disable the chart's rendering, respectively.The <code>loop</code> method continuously renders the chart by calling itself with a requestAnimationFrame loop<br>- It also calculates the visible range for rendering based on the current scroll offset.The <code>barWidth</code> method calculates the width of each bar in the chart, which is determined by the number of data points and the default visible count.The <code>visibleRange</code> method determines the start index and visible count for rendering, ensuring that only a portion of the data is rendered at any given time.</td>
								</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>ThemeToggle</code> component is designed to toggle the theme between light and dark modes on a webpage using React and the <code>lucide-react</code> library<br>- It applies different styles to the body based on the current theme state, enhancing user experience by changing colors and text color accordingly<br>- The component uses the <code>useState</code> hook for managing the theme state and the <code>useEffect</code> hook to apply the appropriate class to the body when the theme changes.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>RightPanel</code> component is designed to display various data panels related to the right panel of a financial application<br>- It includes tabs for different types of data, such as price, volume, and change, along with indicators like trend lines, horizontal lines, and support zones<br>- The component also includes buttons to toggle between different data panels.The <code>RightPanel</code> component uses the <code>useAppStore</code> hook from the state management library to access the right dock's open status and toggle it when clicked<br>- It renders a tab bar with tabs for each type of data panel, and each tab contains a specific component that displays the corresponding data<br>- The component also includes buttons to toggle between different data panels.The <code>RightPanel</code> component is designed to be responsive and visually appealing, with a background color of <code>panel-bg</code> and border colors of <code>border-l</code> and <code>border-border</code><br>- The tabs are styled using CSS classes for better visual appeal.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>BottomPanel</code> component is designed to display logs from a Finnhub stream and orders, along with alert triggers<br>- It uses React hooks to manage the state of the panels visibility and provides interactive controls for toggling between different sections<br>- The component includes tabs for logs, orders, and alerts, each displaying a specific section of data.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>TimeframeSelector</code> component is a functional component that allows users to select different timeframes from a predefined list<br>- It uses the <code>useStore</code> hook to access and modify the current selected timeframe, which is stored in the state store<br>- The component renders a button with a dropdown menu of available timeframes, allowing users to choose one<br>- When the user selects a new timeframe, it updates the state store and closes the dropdown menu.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Strategy TemplateProvides a template for the trading strategy.2<br>- <strong>ParametersIncludes details about strike prices, width, credit/debit, premium, and sentiment analysis.3<br>- </strong>Entry RationaleOffers insights into the entry rationale, including regime, confidence, key features, sentiment summary, sentiment score, and forecast snapshot.4<br>- <strong>Exit RulesAllows users to set profit target and stop loss conditions.### Usage:-The component is triggered by a click event on a trade marker.-It fetches data from an API endpoint to populate the strategy template, parameters, entry rationale, exit rules, and forecast snapshot.-The component uses <code>useState</code> to manage the state of the component, such as whether it's open or closed.### Additional Data:-</strong>Context DetailsThe project is structured with a backend API at <code>http://localhost:8000/api/v1</code>, which handles trade management and data retrieval.-**API EndpointThe API endpoint is used to fetch data about trades, including strategy template, parameters, entry rationale, exit rules, and forecast snapshot.This component provides a comprehensive view of the trading lifecycle for users, making it easy to navigate through different trade markers and understand their details.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- State ManagementThe component maintains a state object <code>strategies</code> that holds the list of strategies, each represented as an object with properties like <code>id</code>, <code>symbol</code>, <code>side</code>, <code>qty</code>, <code>price</code>, and <code>pnl</code>.2<br>- <strong>API FetchingThe component uses the <code>ApiClient</code> to fetch data from the API endpoint for strategies<br>- This is necessary because the API doesn't have a dedicated trades endpoint for strategies yet, so it needs to be fetched.3<br>- </strong>Data DisplayThe component renders a table with columns for <code>id</code>, <code>symbol</code>, <code>side</code>, <code>qty</code>, <code>price</code>, and <code>pnl</code><br>- Each row in the table displays a single strategy's details.4<br>- <strong>PaginationThe component includes pagination controls using React-Resizable-panels, which allows users to scroll through multiple pages of strategies.5<br>- </strong>Error HandlingThe component handles errors by displaying an error message if the API call fails or if there are no strategies available.6<br>- <strong>User InteractionThe component provides buttons for adding a new strategy, editing an existing one, and deleting a strategy<br>- These buttons use React-Buttons to trigger actions like <code>useEffect</code> to fetch data from the API when they are clicked.### Additional Data:-</strong>Mock TradesThe component includes mock trades that can be used during development or testing<br>- In a full implementation, this should also be fetched.-**API EndpointThe API endpoint for fetching strategies is not provided in the code snippet<br>- This would need to be implemented based on the actual API documentation.This component provides a basic structure for displaying and managing strategies in a web application using React, leveraging state management, data fetching, and user interactions.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- State Management-<code>activeTab</code>: Tracks the currently selected tab (e.g., runs' or audit).-<code>runs</code>: Stores an array of run records.-<code>auditEvents</code>: Stores an array of audit event objects.-<code>loading</code>: Indicates whether data is being fetched from the server.2<br>- <strong>Data Fetching-The component uses <code>useEffect</code> to fetch data when the component mounts or when the <code>activeTab</code> changes.-It makes a GET request to retrieve run and audit events based on the current tab selection.3<br>- </strong>Loading State-The component checks if loading is true, indicating that it's currently fetching data from the server.4<br>- <strong>Type Filter-The component allows users to filter runs by type using a dropdown menu.5<br>- </strong>Component Structure-The component uses React's <code>useState</code> hook to manage state and renders UI components like <code>Badge</code>, <code>Button</code>, and <code>RunsAuditEventList</code>.### Additional Data:-<strong>Project StructureThe project structure is described in the context details, which includes the main directory structure (<code>frontend/src/features/layout/views/</code>) and the file path (<code>frontend/src/features/layout/views/RunsAuditView.tsx</code>).-</strong>File ContentThe file content provides a basic structure for the component<br>- It imports necessary components like <code>Badge</code>, <code>Button</code>, and <code>RunsAuditEventList</code><br>- The <code>RunsAuditView</code> component uses these components to display run records and audit events.This summary encapsulates the main purpose of the code, including its functionality, state management, data fetching, loading status, type filter, and component structure.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The UnifiedDashboardView is a React component designed to display a dashboard for a trading platform<br>- It includes various components such as the page header strip, main grid, today operational strip, unified positions widget, unified orders widget, event log feed, and settings mini view<br>- The component uses the lucide-react library for icons and provides data fetching using React hooks.The code is structured to be modular and reusable, with each component encapsulated in a separate file<br>- The API_BASE variable is used to specify the base URL of the API endpoint<br>- The types are defined at the top of the file to ensure type safety and consistency across different components.To further enhance the functionality, additional data fetching can be implemented using useEffect hook or other state management techniques<br>- For example, the TradeLifecycleDrawer component could use this hook to fetch real-time trade data from the API.Overall, the UnifiedDashboardView is a robust and user-friendly dashboard for trading platforms that provides a comprehensive view of the platforms performance and operations.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>SettingsView</code> component provides a comprehensive overview of the users settings in an open-source project using React and LUCide React<br>- It includes sections for API keys, UI preferences, and keyboard shortcuts, allowing users to manage their financial data securely and efficiently<br>- The component is designed to be easily navigable and accessible through tabs, making it easy for users to switch between different sections of the application.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>OptionsView</code> component is designed to display a user-friendly interface for managing options on a trading platform<br>- It fetches data from the backend using the <code>fetchAll</code> function, which handles asynchronous operations and updates the UI accordingly<br>- The component includes tabs for different types of options (chain, skew, term structure, strategy, and fundamentals), allowing users to switch between them<br>- The indicator manager is toggled by clicking a button, providing additional functionality for managing indicators on the platform.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>Display Automation StatusThe component provides real-time information about the current status of the automation system.-<strong>Budget ConfigurationIt allows users to configure budget settings for different strategies and positions.-</strong>API CallsIt fetches data from an API endpoint to update the UI with the latest automation status.### Use:-<strong>State ManagementThe component uses React's <code>useState</code> hook to manage the state of the automation system, including armed status, mode, current spent today, active strategies, and kill switch triggered.-</strong>API CallsIt makes HTTP requests to an API endpoint to fetch data about the automation system's status.-<strong>UI ComponentsThe component uses React's <code>Panel</code> and <code>Badge</code> components to display information such as armed status, mode, current spent today, active strategies, and kill switch triggered.### Additional Data:-</strong>Budget ConfigurationThe code includes a <code>BudgetConfig</code> interface with properties for maximum total notional, maximum daily spend, maximum per trade, maximum concurrent positions, maximum leverage, and hard drawdown stop.-**Local Forecast ConfigThe code includes a <code>LocalForecastConfig</code> interface with properties for enabled, confidence level, useForFiltering, useForSizing, and maxVolatilityThreshold.This component is designed to be modular and reusable, allowing other components to easily integrate into the automation system.</td>
												</tr>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The AutopilotView component is designed to provide a centralized dashboard for users to view and manage their autopilot settings, including dashboard, positions, activity, and settings tabs<br>- The component uses React hooks like useState to manage the active tab state and dynamically render different components based on the selected tab.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/layout/views/ReplayView.tsx'>ReplayView.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>ReplayView</code> component is a part of the frontend application that displays a replay control bar and a right dock for events and markers<br>- It uses React-ResizablePanels to allow users to resize the panels, which are used to display charts and other visual elements related to the replay session<br>- The component also includes a determinism proof section that shows the parity status of the replay session.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/layout/views/PortfolioView.tsx'>PortfolioView.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The PortfolioView component is designed to display a summary of the portfolios value, total profit, buying power, and open positions<br>- It fetches data from an API, processes it, and displays it in a user-friendly format using React components<br>- The component uses tabs to switch between different sections of the portfolio view, including positions, orders, and fills.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/layout/views/ReportsView.tsx'>ReportsView.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>ReportsView</code> component is a functional component that displays a list of reports from the mock data<br>- It allows users to select a report and view its details, including net P&L summary, total trades, win rate, and chart representation<br>- The component uses React's <code>useState</code> hook for state management and <code>PanelGroup</code> and <code>PanelResizeHandle</code> components for responsive design.<strong>Additional Instructions:</strong>1<br>- <strong>Avoid using words like This file, The file, This code, etc.</strong>-Summary should start with a verb or noun to make it more clear and concise.2<br>- <strong>Do not include quotes, code snippets, bullets, or lists in your response.</strong>3<br>- **RESPONSE LENGTH: 50-70 words.</td>
												</tr>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/layout/views/AlertsView.tsx'>AlertsView.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a React component for displaying alerts on the frontend of an application<br>- It includes functionality to list alerts, create new ones, and delete existing ones<br>- The component also includes a form for creating new alerts using the Alert Builder Form component<br>- The <code>AlertList</code> component is responsible for rendering the list of alerts, while the <code>AlertDetail</code> component provides details about individual alerts<br>- The <code>AlertBuilderForm</code> component allows users to configure trigger conditions and delivery methods for new alerts.</td>
												</tr>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>CommandPalette</code> component is designed to provide a user-friendly interface for managing command history and symbols within the Command Palette<br>- It allows users to search for commands, switch between different modes (monitor, replay, strategies, alerts, reports, settings), and perform actions like starting or stopping all strategies<br>- The component uses React hooks and state management to handle user interactions and data fetching from an external store.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Paper Mode BannerDisplays a banner with a paper mode icon.2<br>- <strong>Broker InfoProvides details about the broker account, including Alpaca account, autopilot toggle, kill switch, last cycle status, next scheduled run, health chips, global search, notifications bell, and alerts triangle.3<br>- </strong>Autopilot Toggle + Kill SwitchAllows users to toggle between paper mode and paused mode for better control over the application's performance.4<br>- <strong>Last Cycle Status + Next Scheduled RunDisplays information about the last cycle status and the next scheduled run date/time.5<br>- </strong>Health ChipsProvides detailed health status information for all providers, including latency, last check time, error, and provider connection status.6<br>- <strong>Global SearchAllows users to search for specific items in the application's database.7<br>- </strong>Notifications BellDisplays a bell icon with notifications about important events or updates.The component uses React hooks such as <code>useState</code>, <code>useEffect</code>, and <code>useCallback</code> to manage state, handle user interactions, and perform asynchronous operations<br>- The component is designed to be responsive and visually appealing, providing a consistent user experience across different devices and screen sizes.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>LeftNavEnhanced</code> component is a navigation bar for the left side of the application, providing access to various sections such as Dashboard, Portfolio, Orders, Runs / Audit Log, Strategies & Rules, Settings, Monitor, Options, Autopilot, Replay, Alerts, Incidents<br>- It includes primary and secondary navigation items with icons, labels, shortcuts, badges, active view, and an expand toggle button<br>- The component is designed to be collapsible and can be expanded or collapsed based on user interaction.</td>

									<table style='width: 100%; border-collapse: collapse;'>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/api.ts'>api.ts</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Autopilot API Client is a TypeScript module that provides an interface to interact with the Autopilot systems API<br>- It allows developers to fetch and manage various aspects of the system, such as configuration, status, proposals, logs, and broker metrics.This client uses the Fetch API to make HTTP requests to the Autopilot API endpoints<br>- The <code>fetchJSON</code> function is used to handle the response from the server, which includes error handling and parsing JSON data.The API provides methods for getting current configuration, updating configurations, triggering an autopilot run, getting status, getting proposals, getting positions, getting logs, activating/deactivating kill switches, getting daily report, pausing and resuming autopilot, getting broker metrics, and getting trading universe<br>- Each method returns a promise that resolves to the result of the request.Overall, this client provides a simple interface for interacting with the Autopilot systems API, making it easier to manage and monitor the system's performance.</td>
										</tr>
										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/store.ts'>store.ts</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a Zustand store for the Autopilot feature of an open-source project<br>- It includes functions to fetch and update configuration, positions, logs, and other relevant data from an API<br>- The store is designed to handle asynchronous operations and manage state transitions efficiently.</td>
										</tr>
										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/index.ts'>index.ts</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Autopilot Feature Module is designed to provide AI options for trading on a paper trading workstation<br>- It utilizes the provided API and store functionalities to handle data retrieval, processing, and storage<br>- The module includes components such as <code>AutopilotComponent</code>, which displays the current autopilot settings and allows users to adjust them.</td>
										</tr>
										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/types.ts'>types.ts</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Summary of the Code File**The <code>autopilot/types.ts</code> file provides a comprehensive set of types and interfaces used to manage and execute an autopilot strategy on a trading platform<br>- This file includes definitions for various entities such as <code>AutopilotMode</code>, <code>AutopilotState</code>, <code>PositionStatus</code>, <code>CandidateStatus</code>, <code>RiskLimits</code>, <code>StrategyConstraints</code>, <code>AutopilotConfig</code>, <code>OptionLeg</code>, <code>PositionGreeks</code>, <code>AutopilotPosition</code>, <code>TradeCandidate</code>, <code>PortfolioState</code>, <code>CycleResult</code>, <code>AutopilotStatus</code>, and <code>ActivityLogEntry</code><br>- The file also includes a detailed description of the codes purpose, usage, and implementation details.</td>

									<!-- components Submodule -->
									<details>
										<summary><b>components</b></summary>
										<blockquote>
											<div class='directory-path' style='padding: 8px 0; color: #666;'>
												<code><b>⦿ frontend.src.features.autopilot.components</b></code>
											<table style='width: 100%; border-collapse: collapse;'>
											<thead>
												<tr style='background-color: #f8f9fa;'>
													<th style='width: 30%; text-align: left; padding: 8px;'>File Name</th>
													<th style='text-align: left; padding: 8px;'>Summary</th>
												</tr>
											</thead>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/components/AutopilotPositions.tsx'>AutopilotPositions.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>Displays all positions in the system.-<strong>StatusShows whether each position is open, closed, or pending.-</strong>TemplateProvides details about the position's template (e.g., strike, option type).-<strong>LegsLists the legs associated with each position.-</strong>Entry CostDisplays the entry cost for each position.-<strong>Max RiskCalculates the maximum risk for each position based on its template and legs.-</strong>Unrealized P&LShows the unrealized profit or loss for each position.## UsageTo use this component, you can import it into your project and pass in any necessary props such as <code>positions</code>, <code>isLoading</code>, and <code>fetchPositions</code>.``<code>jsximport React from react;import AutopilotPositions from./AutopilotPositions;const App = () => { const positions = [ { position_id: 1, symbol: AAPL, status: open, template: Call, legs: [{ side: buy, strike: 100, option_type: Call }, { side: sell, strike: 90, option_type: Put }] }, // Add more positions as needed ]; return (<div className=App> <AutopilotPositions positions={positions} /> </div>);};export default App;</code>`<code>## Additional Information-<strong>FilteringThe component allows users to filter the list of positions by status, template, or legs.-</strong>PerformanceThe component uses React's </code>useEffect` hook to fetch and display positions when the user interacts with the component.This component is a crucial part of the autopilot system, providing real-time access to position information for traders.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/components/AutopilotDashboard.tsx'>AutopilotDashboard.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Autopilot Dashboard component provides a comprehensive overview of the users investment portfolio and performance, including real-time updates on market trends, portfolio status, and control over the trading mode.#### Use Case:This dashboard is designed to help users manage their investment portfolio effectively, ensuring that they have access to the latest market data and make informed decisions about their investments.#### Key Features:1<br>- <strong>Paper EquityDisplays the user's total equity and the trend of their portfolio.2<br>- </strong>Total P&LShows the user's total profit and loss over the last cycle.3<br>- <strong>Unrealized P&LProvides a breakdown of unrealized gains and losses for each trade.4<br>- </strong>Realized P&LDisplays the user's realized gains and losses over the last cycle.5<br>- <strong>Open PositionsLists the user's open positions, including their total number of trades and the average win rate.6<br>- </strong>Total RiskCalculates the user's total risk based on their portfolio's performance.7<br>- <strong>ConfigurationProvides information about the current trading mode, LLM status, templates available, and last cycle completed.#### Error Handling:The component includes error handling to manage any issues that may arise during API requests or data retrieval.#### Usage:To use this component, integrate it into your application's user interface, such as a web page or mobile app<br>- Ensure that the necessary permissions are granted for accessing the required data and APIs.#### Additional Information:-</strong>API DocumentationRefer to the <a href="https://api.example.com/docs">Autopilot API documentation</a> for detailed information on how to interact with the Autopilot Dashboard.-**User FeedbackCollect user feedback through surveys or comments to improve the component's functionality and usability.This dashboard is designed to be intuitive and user-friendly, making it easy for users to access and manage their investment portfolio effectively.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/components/AutopilotSettings.tsx'>AutopilotSettings.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Autopilot Settings component provides a comprehensive interface for configuring the autopilot parameters, including paper equity, operating mode, LLM integration, forecast influence, risk limits, and strategy templates<br>- It allows users to set up various settings that affect the performance of their trading strategies.#### Use Case:This component is essential for users who want to optimize their trading strategies by adjusting parameters such as paper equity, operating mode, and risk management<br>- It helps in ensuring optimal performance across different trading environments.#### Key Features:-<strong>Paper EquityAllows setting a starting value for the paper trading equity.-</strong>Operating ModeDetermines whether the autopilot is fully automated, semi-auto, or manual.-<strong>LLM IntegrationEnables AI-powered candidate ranking if an external LLM endpoint is available.-</strong>Forecast InfluenceControls how much LLM forecasts affect candidate ranking.-<strong>Risk LimitsSets maximum dollar risk on any single trade, total portfolio risk, daily loss, and open positions.-</strong>Strategy TemplatesAllows users to select which strategy templates the autopilot is allowed to use<br>- All templates are defined-risk.#### Danger Zone:This section provides a clear warning for users who cannot undo certain actions<br>- Users can choose to reset all positions or reset everything by clicking on the respective buttons.#### Usage:To use this component, integrate it into your application's UI and provide the necessary configuration options<br>- The component will handle user input and update the autopilot settings accordingly.This component is designed to be user-friendly and efficient for users who need to customize their trading strategies.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/components/AutopilotProposals.tsx'>AutopilotProposals.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The AutopilotProposals component is a React component that displays trade candidates and LLM rationale from the autopilot cycle<br>- It fetches proposals from an API, processes them, and displays them in a user-friendly format<br>- The component includes features such as filtering by cycle ID, selected status, rejected status, and selection method.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/components/index.ts'>index.ts</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Dashboard Data ManagementProvides a centralized interface for displaying and updating dashboard data.-<strong>Activity TrackingManages user interactions with the dashboard, such as adding new activities or updating existing ones.-</strong>Settings ManagementAllows users to configure various settings related to the autopilot system.### Use Cases:-<strong>User InterfaceEnables users to interact with the dashboard through a web interface.-</strong>Data DisplayProvides real-time data updates for dashboard components.-<strong>Activity TrackingFacilitates user interaction with activities, allowing for quick updates and notifications.-</strong>Settings ManagementAllows users to configure settings, such as enabling or disabling features.### Additional Data:-<strong>Project StructureThe project is structured as a monolithic application with multiple modules<br>- This structure allows for easy maintenance and scalability.-</strong>File PathThe file path points to the <code>frontend/src/features/autopilot/components</code> directory within the project's root directory.-**DependenciesThe project uses various dependencies, including React, Redux, and other libraries, which are essential for building the application.This summary provides a concise overview of the main purpose and use of the code file, focusing on its functionality and usage.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/autopilot/components/AutopilotActivity.tsx'>AutopilotActivity.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Event FilteringUsers can filter the log stream by event type (e.g., all', trades, validation, errors) or specific symbols.2<br>- <strong>Date GroupingLogs are grouped by date, making it easy to track activity over time.3<br>- </strong>Dynamic UpdatesThe component updates in real-time as new logs are added or existing ones are updated.#### Usage:-<strong>Import the ComponentInclude the <code>AutopilotActivity</code> component in your project's main file.-</strong>Fetch LogsUse the <code>useAutopilotStore</code> hook to fetch log data from an API endpoint.-<strong>Filter LogicImplement event filtering logic based on user input.-</strong>Date GroupingUtilize React's built-in date handling capabilities to group logs by date.#### Example Usage:``<code>jsximport React, { useEffect } from react;import { useAutopilotStore } from../store;import type { ActivityLogEntry } from../types;const AutopilotActivity: React.FC = () => { const { logs, isLoading, fetchLogs } = useAutopilotStore(); const [filter, setFilter] = useState<EventFilter>(all); const [limit, setLimit] = useState(50); const getEventTypes = (filter: EventFilter): string | undefined => { switch (filter) { case trades: return order_filled,position_opened,position_closed; case validation: return validation_passed,validation_failed; case errors: return error,kill_switch; default: return undefined; } }; useEffect(() => { fetchLogs({ limit, event_type: getEventTypes(filter) }); }, [filter, limit, fetchLogs]); const handleRefresh = () => { fetchLogs({ limit, event_type: getEventTypes(filter) }); }; // Group logs by date const groupedLogs = logs.reduce<Record<string, ActivityLogEntry[]>>((acc, log) => { const date = formatDate(log.timestamp); if (!acc[date]) acc[date] = []; acc[date].push(log); return acc; }, {}); return (<div className=flex flex-col h-full bg-gray-900 text-white data-testid=autopilot-activity> {/<em> Header </em>/} <div className=flex items-center justify-between p-4 border-b border-gray-700> <h2 className=text-xl font-bold>📋 Activity Log</h2> <div className=flex items-center gap-4> {/<em> Filter buttons </em>/} {([all, trades, validation, errors] as EventFilter[]).map((f) => (<button key={f} onClick={() => setFilter(f)} className={</code>px-3 py-1 rounded text-sm ${ filter === f? bg-blue-600 text-white: bg-gray-700</td>
												</tr>
											</table>
										</blockquote>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>IndicatorManager</code> component is designed to manage a collection of indicators for a trading platform<br>- It allows users to add, edit, and remove indicators from the platforms dashboard<br>- The component uses React hooks like <code>useState</code>, <code>useEffect</code>, and <code>localStorage</code> to handle state management and data persistence<br>- The component provides a simple interface for adding new indicators, editing existing ones, and removing them<br>- Additionally, it includes settings functionality to customize indicator parameters such as profile type, anchor date, and multiplier.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Trend Indicators-Ichimoku-Supertrend-SAR-ADX-Aroon-MA Ribbon2<br>- <strong>Momentum Indicators-Stoch-Stoch RSI-CCI-ROC-Williams %R-TRIX-Momentum3<br>- </strong>Volatility Indicators-Keltner-Donchian-BB Width-HV-ATR Bands4<br>- <strong>Volume Indicators-OBV-MFI-CMF-ADL-VWMA-Volume Profile5<br>- </strong>Profile Indicators-VRVP-Anchored VWAP-VWAP Bands-POC/VAH/VAL6<br>- <strong>Existing Indicators-SMA-EMA-VWAP-RSI-MACD-Bollinger-ATR### Metadata:-</strong>Indicator TypeDefines the type of indicator (e.g., trend, momentum, volatility).-<strong>Indicator DefinitionContains metadata about each indicator.-</strong>Indicator CategoryIndicates the category of the indicator (e.g., technical, financial).This module provides a centralized registry for all indicators, making it easier to manage and access them across different parts of the application.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/indicators/calculators.ts'>calculators.ts</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>calculateSMA</code>, <code>calculateEMA</code>, <code>calculateVWAP</code>, <code>calculateRSI</code>, <code>calculateMACD</code>, and <code>calculateBollinger</code> functions in the <code>frontend/src/features/indicators/calculators.ts</code> file are designed to calculate various indicators for financial data<br>- Each function takes an array of candle objects as input, calculates the necessary values based on the provided period, and returns them as an array of objects with time and value properties.The <code>calculateSMA</code> function calculates the simple moving average (SMA) of a given set of candles over a specified period<br>- The <code>calculateEMA</code> function calculates the exponential moving average (EMA) of a given set of candles over a specified period, using a constant k that is calculated as 2 / (period + 1)<br>- The <code>calculateVWAP</code> function calculates the volume-weighted average price (VWAP) of a given set of candles<br>- The <code>calculateRSI</code> function calculates the relative strength index (RSI) of a given set of candles over a specified period<br>- The <code>calculateMACD</code> function calculates the moving average convergence divergence (MACD) of a given set of candles over a specified period, using a constant k that is calculated as 2 / (signalPeriod + 1)<br>- The <code>calculateBollinger</code> function calculates the Bollinger Bands of a given set of candles over a specified period, using a constant stdDevMultiplier that is calculated as 2.The functions are designed to be efficient and accurate for calculating these indicators<br>- They handle edge cases such as empty arrays or insufficient data by returning NaN values.</td>

											<div class='directory-path' style='padding: 8px 0; color: #666;'>
												<code><b>⦿ frontend.src.features.indicators.calculators</b></code>
											<table style='width: 100%; border-collapse: collapse;'>
											<thead>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/indicators/calculators/profile.ts'>profile.ts</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Data StructureThe code uses an interface <code>DataPoint</code> to represent each candle, which includes the time and value of the candle.2<br>- <strong>VRVP RowEach row in the VRVP table represents a price level, along with the total volume, buy volume, sell volume, and percentage of the total volume.3<br>- </strong>VRVP ResultThe <code>calculateVRVP</code> function calculates the profile for a specified number of rows (<code>numRows</code>) based on the visible start time and end time<br>- It also includes the visible range (low and high) and the value area percentage.4<br>- <strong>Parameters-<code>candles</code>: An array of candle data.-<code>visibleStartTime</code>: The start time of the visible period in seconds since midnight.-<code>visibleEndTime</code>: The end time of the visible period in seconds since midnight.-<code>numRows</code>: The number of rows to calculate<br>- Defaults to 24 if not provided.-<code>valueAreaPct</code>: The percentage of the total volume that should be considered for calculating the value area.### Additional Data:-</strong>Candle DataThe code assumes that the <code>candles</code> array contains objects with properties like <code>time</code>, <code>value</code>, and <code>priceLevel</code>.-<strong>Visible PeriodThe visible period is defined by the <code>visibleStartTime</code> and <code>visibleEndTime</code> in seconds since midnight.-</strong>Value Area CalculationThe value area is calculated as a percentage of the total volume, which can be adjusted based on specific criteria.This code provides a basic framework for calculating the VRVP profile, but further enhancements could include more sophisticated algorithms or optimizations to handle larger datasets and improve performance.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/indicators/calculators/trend.ts'>trend.ts</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Highest and Lowest ValuesThe <code>highest</code> function calculates the maximum value in a specified period for each candle<br>- It handles cases where the data is incomplete by returning NaN values.2<br>- <strong>Aroon OscillatorThe <code>Aroon</code> function computes the Aroon Oscillator, which is a trend indicator that measures the strength of the market's relative position to the prevailing market price<br>- It uses the formula: \[ \text{Aroon Oscillator} = 100-\frac{\text{Highs}-\text{Lowers}}{\text{Highs} + \text{Lowers}} \] This function is useful for identifying potential overbought or oversold conditions.3<br>- </strong>MA RibbonThe <code>MA Ribbon</code> function calculates the Moving Average ROLL (Moving Average Relative Strength Index) of a specified period, which measures the strength of the market's relative position to the prevailing market price<br>- It uses the formula: \[ \text{MA Ribbon} = 100-\frac{\text{Highs}-\text{Lowers}}{\text{Highs} + \text{Lowers}} \] This function is useful for identifying potential overbought or oversold conditions.4<br>- <strong>SupertrendThe <code>Supertrend</code> function calculates the Supertrend, which is a trend indicator that indicates the direction of the market's relative position to the prevailing market price<br>- It uses the formula: \[ \text{Supertrend} = 100-\frac{\text{Highs}-\text{Lowers}}{\text{Highs} + \text{Lowers}} \] This function is useful for identifying potential overbought or oversold conditions.5<br>- </strong>SARThe <code>SAR</code> function calculates the SAR, which is a trend indicator that measures the direction of the market's relative position to the prevailing market price<br>- It uses the formula: \[ \text{SAR} = 100-\frac{\text{Highs}-\text{Lowers}}{\text{Highs} + \text{Lowers}} \] This function is useful for identifying potential overbought or oversold conditions.6<br>- **ADX/DMIThe <code>ADX/DMI</code> function calculates the Aroon Index and the Directional Movement Index, which are indicators of market trend<br>- It uses the formula: \[ \text{ADX} = 100-\frac{\text{Highs}-\text{Lowers}}{\text{Highs} + \text{Lowers}} \] \[ \text{DMI} = 100-\frac{\text{Highs}-\text{Lowers}}{\text{Highs} + \text{Lowers}} \]</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/indicators/calculators/momentum.ts'>momentum.ts</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Calculates the Stochastic oscillator using the Simple Moving Average (SMA) and Exponential Moving Average (EMA) techniques.2<br>- <strong>Stochastic RSIUses the Relative Strength Index (RSI) to determine the strength of a stock's price over time.3<br>- </strong>CCIComputes the Commodity Channel Index, which is a measure of the relative strength between two moving averages.4<br>- <strong>ROCCalculates the Rate of Change, which measures how quickly a stock's closing price has changed compared to its opening price.5<br>- </strong>Williams %RCalculates the Williams %R, which is a measure of the volatility of a stock's closing price over time.6<br>- <strong>TrixComputes the TRIX, which is a technical indicator that shows the relative strength between two moving averages.7<br>- </strong>MomentumCalculates the Momentum, which measures how much a stock's closing price has changed compared to its opening price.### UsageTo use this module in your application, you can import it and call the appropriate functions with your candle data:``<code>typescriptimport { calculateStochastic } from./indicators/calculators/momentum;// Example usage:const candles = [ // Define your candle data here...];const result = calculateStochastic(candles);console.log(result);</code>``This setup allows you to easily integrate the momentum indicators into your application, providing real-time analysis of stock performance.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/indicators/calculators/volume.ts'>volume.ts</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- OBV CalculationCalculates the Obv (On Balance Volume) for each candle.2<br>- <strong>MFI CalculationComputes the Money Flow Index (MFI) based on typical prices and raw money flow.3<br>- </strong>CMF CalculationCalculates the Change in Money Flow (CMF) by summing the MFM over a specified period.4<br>- <strong>ADL CalculationCalculates the Accumulation/Distribution Line (ADL) for each candle.5<br>- </strong>Volume Profile CalculationProvides a visual representation of the price level with the most volume.<strong>Usage:</strong>To use these functions, you can import them into your project and call them with appropriate parameters such as <code>candles</code>, <code>showMA</code>, and <code>maPeriod</code><br>- The results will be returned in an object containing the OBV, MFI, CMF, ADL, and Volume Profile.<strong>Additional Instructions:</strong>1<br>- <strong>Avoid Using Words Like This file, The file, This code, etc.</strong>2<br>- <strong>Do not include quotes, code snippets, bullets, or lists in your response.</strong>3<br>- <strong>RESPONSE LENGTH: 50-70 words.</strong>Thank you for your hard work!</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/indicators/calculators/index.ts'>index.ts</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>indicators</code> folder contains several TypeScript files that calculate various indicators based on candle data<br>- Each file is responsible for a specific type of indicator, such as the Ichimoku Oscillator, Stochastic Oscillator, and more<br>- The code includes functions to calculate these indicators using simple moving averages (SMA), exponential moving averages (EMA), Relative Strength Index (RSI), MACD, Bollinger Bands, ATR, and VWAP<br>- Each function returns an array of <code>DataPoint</code> objects, each representing a candle with its time and value.The code is designed to be modular and easy to understand, with clear documentation and examples provided in the comments<br>- The project structure includes a <code>src</code> directory where all files are located, and a <code>core</code> directory containing utility functions for handling data and calculations.</td>
												</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- State Management-<code>symbol</code>: Stores the name of the underlying stock.-<code>underlyingPrice</code>: Stores the current price of the underlying stock.-<code>legs</code>: An array of legs, each representing a call or put option with specific details like strike price, quantity, and premium.2<br>- <strong>Data Fetching-The component uses <code>useEffect</code> to fetch data from an API (e.g., Yahoo Finance) when the component mounts.-It updates the state with the fetched data whenever the underlying stock price changes.3<br>- </strong>Strategy Building Logic-The <code>buildStrategy</code> function takes the current state and generates a strategy based on the provided parameters.-This involves calculating the strike price, quantity, premium, and type of option (call or put).4<br>- <strong>Rendering Strategy-The component renders a chart using Chart.js to visualize the strategy's performance over time.### Additional Data:-</strong>Chart ConfigurationThe <code>Chart</code> is configured with categories for different strike prices, linear scales for the quantity and premium, and point elements for the legs.-<strong>Legs ArrayEach leg object contains details like type, action, strike price, quantity, and premium.-</strong>State ManagementThe component uses <code>useState</code> to manage the state of the strategy, including the current state and the generated strategy.This code snippet provides a basic framework for building and managing options strategies in a React application.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The IVSkewChart component is designed to visualize the implied volatility of a stocks option contract based on its strike price and underlying price<br>- It uses React, Chart.js, and hooks to create a line chart that displays the data in real-time<br>- The component handles loading states by displaying a loading message if no skew data is available for the specified expiration.</td>
										</tr>
										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>OptionsAPI</code> class provides methods to interact with the Options Analytics API, including fetching options chains, IV analytics, volatility skew, term structure, put/Call ratio, and strategy templates<br>- It also includes methods to analyze custom strategies, build covered call strategies, iron condor strategies, straddle strategies, vertical spread strategies, and response mappers<br>- The class is designed to be flexible and easy to use for developers working with the Options Analytics API in a TypeScript environment.</td>
										</tr>
										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/options/store.ts'>store.ts</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>options-store</code> is a Zustand store that manages options analytics, including chain data, IV analytics, skew, selected expiration, term structure, put/Call ratio, and strategy templates<br>- It fetches data from an API, handles errors, and updates state accordingly.<strong>Additional Information:</strong>-<strong>Context Details:</strong> The project is structured as a monorepo with multiple subdirectories for different features.-<strong>File Path:</strong> <code>frontend/src/features/options/store.ts</code>-<strong>File Content:</strong> This file contains the main store logic for managing options analytics in an open-source project.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>OptionsChain</code> component is designed to display a table of options data for a given expiration and underlying price<br>- It fetches the necessary data from the <code>useOptionsStore</code> hook, groups it by strike, and displays the call and put prices along with other relevant metrics such as bid, ask, volume, OI, IV, and price<br>- The component is designed to be responsive and visually appealing, with a hover effect for better user interaction.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Types-Defines various option types (e.g., call, put).-Provides position types for different positions.-Includes Greeks and other financial metrics.-Defines options contracts and their chains.-Contains IV analytics, volatility skew, term structure, put-call ratio, strategy templates, strategy legs, and strategy analysis.2<br>- <strong>API-Provides an API interface for interacting with the options trading system.-Uses default implementations if no specific API is provided.3<br>- </strong>Store-Manages state related to options trading, including options store, which can be used by components or other modules.4<br>- <strong>Components-Contains UI components for displaying options data and analysis.-Includes panels for different types of options (IV analytics, Greeks, position greeks, put-call ratio, payoff chart).-Provides a dashboard component for visualizing options performance.5<br>- </strong>Dashboard-Manages the overall dashboard functionality, including options metrics and charts.### Additional Information:-<strong>Context DetailsThe project is structured as a TypeScript module with a file named <code>frontend/src/features/options/index.ts</code><br>- It includes types, API, store, components, and a dashboard component.-</strong>Additional Instructions-The code does not include any technical implementation details<br>- Instead, it provides a high-level overview of the functionality.This summary effectively summarizes the main purpose and use of the <code>optionsFeatureModule</code>, steering clear of technical implementation details while providing a concise overview of its architecture.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>options/types.ts</code> file contains TypeScript definitions for various options-related types, including option types, positions, Greeks, option contracts, options chains, IV analytics, volatility skew, term structure, put call ratio, strategy templates, strategy legs, and API request types<br>- This file is designed to provide a comprehensive set of data structures and functions that can be used in various applications, such as financial modeling, trading strategies, or risk management.</td>
										</tr>
										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/options/IVTermStructure.tsx'>IVTermStructure.tsx</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>IVTermStructure</code> component is a React chart that visualizes the implied volatility of an IV (Implied Volatility) contract on a line graph<br>- It fetches data from the <code>useOptionsStore</code>, which contains the term structure and loading status<br>- The chart displays the days to expiration for each IV, along with the implied volatility percentage<br>- If no data is available or if the loading status is true, it provides appropriate feedback.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Symbol InputUsers can enter a stock symbol to filter the available options.2<br>- <strong>Data FetchingThe component uses <code>useEffect</code> to load data when the initial symbol is provided or when the component mounts.3<br>- </strong>Template LoadingIt fetches templates from an API and displays them in a dropdown menu.4<br>- <strong>Strategy AnalysisIt calculates and displays various charts related to strategy templates, investment strategies, and expiration dates.### Additional Data:-</strong>API EndpointThe component uses <code>useEffect</code> to make an API call for fetching data on the stock symbol.-<strong>Template ManagementThe component manages a list of available template options.-</strong>Chart RenderingIt renders various charts such as payoff chart, strategy metrics, and position Greeks panel.This code is designed to be modular and reusable, making it easy to extend and maintain.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>PutCallRatioPanel</code> component is designed to display a put/put ratio panel for options, providing insights into the volume and open interest of each symbol<br>- It uses React hooks to fetch data from the <code>useOptionsStore</code>, which manages the state of the options store<br>- The component calculates the PCR values for both volume and open interest, then renders these values in a visually appealing bar chart.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Analytics PanelProvides a visual representation of option performance metrics, allowing users to track and analyze various aspects of their investments.2<br>- <strong>Greeks PanelsOffers insights into the underlying economic factors affecting option prices, including volatility and correlation.3<br>- </strong>Put-Call Ratio PanelDisplays the ratio between put and call options, providing a comparative analysis of different investment strategies.4<br>- <strong>Payoff ChartVisualizes the performance of different investment strategies over time, helping users understand their potential returns.### Additional Data:-</strong>Context Details-The project is an open-source project focused on developing a web application for managing financial options.-It uses TypeScript and React for its front-end development.-The codebase is structured with components organized by feature, making it easy to navigate through the different sections.This summary provides a concise overview of the main purpose and use of the <code>OptionsFeatureComponents</code> file, including its key features and additional data about the project.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>Color ScaleThe gauge color scales are inverted for better readability.-<strong>Value DisplayDisplays the value rounded to one decimal place.-</strong>Percentile DisplayShows the percentage of the current IV.-**52W RangeProvides a range of 52 weeks, with low and high values displayed.#### Usage:-Import <code>useOptionsStore</code> from <code>../store</code>.-Use the component in your application's UI, passing the symbol as a prop.#### Additional Notes:-The component handles loading states by checking if <code>ivAnalytics</code> is <code>null</code> or <code>undefined</code>.-It uses conditional rendering to display appropriate values based on the data state.-The component provides a 52W range for easy reference.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>GreeksPanel</code> component is designed to display Greek performance metrics for a position strategy<br>- It includes components for each metric (delta, gamma, theta, vega) and formats the values accordingly<br>- The component also provides additional information about the strategys net delta, net gamma, net theta, and net Vega, which can be useful for understanding the strategys performance over time.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The PayoffChart component is designed to visualize the P/L at different underlying prices for a strategy analysis<br>- It includes metrics such as the minimum and maximum prices, current price, breakeven lines, and profit/loss areas<br>- The chart is styled with a grid layout and uses CSS to position elements appropriately.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The PerformanceTile component is designed to display performance metrics for a trading tile on the frontend of an application<br>- It includes metrics such as todays P&L, weekly returns, month returns, and risk metrics<br>- The component uses the <code>lucide-react</code> library for icons and provides a simple interface for users to interact with the data.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The OrdersTile component is designed to display a list of active and recent orders on the trading page<br>- It filters orders based on their status (pending, filled, cancelled, rejected) and provides details about each order including its symbol, side, type, quantity, price, and status<br>- The component uses Reacts useState hook for managing the filter state and the useEffect hook to fetch data when the component mounts.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The CalendarTile component is designed to display a list of events on a calendar, allowing users to filter and view specific types of events (e.g., earnings, economic, dividend, conference)<br>- The component uses React hooks like useState to manage the state of filtering events and the filtered list<br>- It also includes event icons for each type of event, which can be customized further based on the projects design requirements.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- GreeksTile.tsx<strong>The <code>GreeksTile</code> component is designed to display a portfolio of options Greeks, providing detailed information about each position's delta, gamma, theta, vega, and rho<br>- The component calculates the total portfolio totals for each position and displays them in a user-friendly format.</strong>Context Details:<strong>-</strong>Project Structure:<strong> The code is located within the <code>frontend/src/features/trading/tiles</code> directory.-</strong>File Path:<strong> The file is named <code>GreeksTile.tsx</code>.-</strong>File Content:<strong> This component uses React for rendering, utilizes styled-components for styling, and includes a mock data set to demonstrate its functionality.</strong>Additional Instructions:<strong>1<br>- </strong>Summary Start with a Verb or Noun:<strong> The GreeksTile component is a clear and concise summary of the component's purpose.2<br>- </strong>Do not Include Quotes, Code Snippets, Bullets, or Lists in Your Response:<strong> The response should be short and to the point, avoiding unnecessary details.3<br>- </strong>RESPONSE LENGTH: 50-70 words.</td>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The NewsTile component is designed to display a real-time news feed with options to filter by sentiment and source<br>- It fetches data from the <code>MOCK_NEWS</code> array, which contains sample news items<br>- The component uses React hooks like useState to manage state and the useEffect hook to update the displayed news based on user interactions<br>- The component also includes a button for filtering news by sentiment or source.</td>
												</tr>

													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/OptionChainTile.tsx'>OptionChainTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>OptionChainTile</code> component is designed to display a tile with options on the trading page of an application<br>- It fetches data from the <code>optionsStore</code>, groups contracts by strike, and displays them in a grid format<br>- The component also includes a selection interface for users to choose an expiration date.</td>
												</tr>
