													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/TimeAndSalesTile.tsx'>TimeAndSalesTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The TimeAndSalesTile component is designed to display real-time trade data from a trading system<br>- It simulates streaming trades and allows users to pause the process, which can be useful for monitoring or analyzing trade patterns<br>- The component includes controls for pausing and resuming the process, as well as a header with time, price, size, side, and exchange information.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/AlertsTile.tsx'>AlertsTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The AlertsTile component is designed to display a list of active price alerts on the frontend<br>- It allows users to toggle and delete these alerts, providing a clear overview of their status<br>- The component uses Reacts useState hook to manage the state of alerts, including enabling or disabling each alert based on user interaction.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/WatchlistTile.tsx'>WatchlistTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>WatchlistTile</code> component is a React functional component that displays a list of watchlists with real-time quotes<br>- It filters the list based on user input, simulates real-time updates using an interval, and provides basic styling for the UI elements<br>- The component uses the <code>useState</code> hook to manage the state of the watchlist, which includes the current search query and the filtered list.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/VolSurfaceTile.tsx'>VolSurfaceTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>VolSurfaceTile</code> component is a React component that visualizes an implied volatility surface for the Apple Inc<br>- stock<br>- The surface includes a grid of strikes and expirations, with each strike corresponding to a different price point<br>- The IV (Implied Volatility) increases with time, and the color of each cell represents the IV value at that time<br>- The legend shows the range of IV values, highlighting the low and high IV levels for each strike.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/index.ts'>index.ts</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>AlertsTile</code>, <code>CalendarTile</code>, <code>ChartTile</code>, <code>GreeksTile</code>, <code>HeatmapTile</code>, <code>NewsTile</code>, <code>OptionChainTile</code>, <code>OrdersTile</code>, <code>PerformanceTile</code>, <code>PositionsTile</code>, <code>ScannerTile</code>, <code>TimeAndSalesTile</code>, and <code>VolSurfaceTile</code> components are part of the trading tiles section of the frontend application<br>- These components provide a user-friendly interface for displaying various types of financial data, including alerts, calendars, charts, Greeks, heatmaps, news articles, option chains, orders, performance metrics, positions, scanners, time and sales, and volume surface<br>- Each component is designed to be reusable and can be easily integrated into the applications layout.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/HeatmapTile.tsx'>HeatmapTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>HeatmapTile</code> component is a React functional component that visualizes a heatmap of market data for a given sector/market<br>- It uses the <code>useMemo</code> hook to sort the data by market cap and then maps over it to render each item as a tile with its symbol, change, and market cap<br>- The color of each tile is determined based on the change in market capital, with green indicating positive changes and red indicating negative changes.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/ChartTile.tsx'>ChartTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>ChartTile</code> component is designed to display a mini chart on the dashboard, allowing users to visualize historical candlestick data<br>- It uses the <code>lightweight-charts</code> library to create and manage the chart, with features like time scale customization and crosshair support<br>- The component is designed to be reusable and can be easily integrated into other parts of the application.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/PositionsTile.tsx'>PositionsTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The PositionsTile component is designed to display a list of open positions with their current price and market value<br>- It includes a summary of the total value and unrealized P&L, as well as a grid of positions with details such as symbol, quantity, price, value, and P&L percentage<br>- The component uses Reacts useState hook for state management and useEffect to update the list of positions in real-time.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/ScannerTile.tsx'>ScannerTile.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>ScannerTile</code> component is designed to display a list of stock screener results, allowing users to filter and view specific symbols based on their price changes<br>- The component includes a set of preset scans for quick access, as well as a dropdown menu for selecting the active scan<br>- The results are displayed in a grid format with columns for symbol, price, change, signal, and strength.</td>
												</tr>
												<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/frontend/src/features/trading/tiles/UncertaintyCone.tsx'>UncertaintyCone.tsx</a></b></td>
													<td style='padding: 8px;'>Chart GenerationUses <code>react-chartjs-2</code> for rendering the chart.-<strong>Data FetchingUtilizes <code>ApiClient.getForecast</code> to fetch historical data for a specified symbol over the last 30 days.-</strong>Interactive ControlsProvides buttons to toggle between displaying the forecast and loading new data.<strong>Dependencies:</strong>-<strong>Chart.jsFor charting.-</strong>Lucide ReactFor UI components.-<strong>AxiosFor API requests.-</strong>State ManagementUtilizes <code>useSymbol</code> from <code>data/ApiClient</code>.<strong>Usage Example:</strong>``<code>jsximport { useState, useEffect } from react;import { TrendingUp, RefreshCw, AlertCircle } from lucide-react;import { Line } from react-chartjs-2;import { Chart as ChartJS, CategoryScale, LinearScale, PointElement, LineElement, Filler, Title, Tooltip, Legend } from chart.js;import type { TooltipItem } from chart.js;import { ApiClient } from../../../data/ApiClient;import type { ForecastResponse } from../../../data/ApiClient;import { Panel } from../../../ui/Panel;import { Button } from../../../ui/Button;// Register Chart.js componentsChartJS.register(CategoryScale, LinearScale, PointElement, LineElement, Filler, Title, Tooltip, Legend);import { useSymbol } from../../../state/appStore;interface TileProps { tileId: string; onClose: () => void; onMaximize: () => void; isMaximized: boolean;}interface UncertaintyConeContentProps { symbol: string; showControls?: boolean;}export function UncertaintyConeContent({ symbol, showControls = true }: UncertaintyConeContentProps) { const days = 30; // Default days for tile view const [forecast, setForecast] = useState<ForecastResponse | null>(null); const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false); const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null); const fetchForecast = useCallback(async () => { setLoading(true); setError(null); try { const data = await ApiClient.getForecast(symbol, days); setForecast(data); } catch (e) { setError((e as Error).message); } finally { setLoading(false); } }, [symbol, days]); useEffect(() => { fetchForecast(); }, [fetchForecast]); // Generate chart data const chartData = forecast? { labels: Array.from({ length: days }, (_, i) => </code>D+${i + 1}`), datasets: [ // 95% confidence-outer cone (light fill) forecast.cones[95%] && { label: 95% Upper, data: forecast.cones[95%].upper, borderColor: rgba(59, 130, 246, 0.3)',</td>
												</tr>
											</table>
										</blockquote>

							<td style='padding: 8px;'>- AM on weekdays (Mon-Fri) with a cron job, sends an HTTP POST request to the Alpaca API for verification results, and logs success or failure based on the number of executed orders and discrepancies<br>- The project uses Node.js, N8n, Docker, and Slack for its architecture.</td>

							<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Daily Report Generator is an automated tool designed to generate reports based on trading activity data from a specified URL<br>- It fetches the latest report and processes it to provide insights into daily performance, including opening positions, total risk, and trading activity<br>- The tool uses N8N for its workflow automation capabilities, which allows for easy integration with various tools and platforms.</td>

							<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Regime Change Handler is an automated webhook that triggers when a regime change occurs in the trading view application<br>- It reduces risk settings based on the new regime, and sends alerts to Slack if the regime changes from high volatility to normal or vice versa<br>- The handler uses N8N nodes for webhooks, HTTP requests, and Slack integration.</td>
						</tr>
						<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/strategies/rsi_breakout.py'>rsi_breakout.py</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The RSI Breakout Strategy is designed to generate buy signals when the RSI falls below an oversold level and sell signals when it rises above an overbought level<br>- The strategy requires a specific trading symbol, period, oversold threshold, and overbought threshold<br>- It also requires a position size for each trade<br>- The strategy uses the <code>rsi</code> function from the <code>..services.strategy.base_strategy</code> module to calculate the RSI<br>- The strategy generates signals based on whether the current RSI is below or above the specified thresholds<br>- The strategy also trims the price history to prevent overloading the system with too many trades.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/strategies/vwap_reversion.py'>vwap_reversion.py</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The VWAP Reversion Strategy is designed to trade mean reversion around the Volume Weighted Average Price (VWAP)<br>- It calculates the current VWAP and standard deviation, then determines when to buy or sell based on whether the price falls below or above the VWAP by a specified threshold<br>- The strategy also includes a mechanism for closing positions when returning to the VWAP, which could be useful for tighter mean reversion.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/strategies/sma_crossover.py'>sma_crossover.py</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>SMA CrossoverStrategy</code> is a simple moving average crossover strategy that buys when the fast SMA crosses above the slow SMA and sells when it crosses below<br>- The strategy uses the <code>sma</code> function from the <code>..services.strategy.base_strategy</code> module to calculate the fast and slow moving averages<br>- It also checks for a flat position by comparing the current price with the previous price and determines whether to buy or sell based on the crossover conditions.</td>
				</tr>
			</table>
		</blockquote>
	</details>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The provided Dockerfile is a basic setup for a Python application using Docker<br>- It includes installing necessary dependencies, copying the application code, creating a non-root user, and setting up the default command to run the application<br>- The file structure and content are straightforward, with the main purpose of providing a base environment for running a Python application.</td>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/pytest.ini'>pytest.ini</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- ConfigurationProvides a structured way to define test settings, such as test directories, Python files, and class names.-<strong>ExecutionAllows users to run tests using <code>pytest</code> from the command line or through IDEs like VSCode or PyCharm.-</strong>Integration TestingEnables integration testing by automatically running tests in multiple environments (e.g., local, remote).-<strong>Parity VerificationEnsures that all test cases are independent and do not depend on each other.### Use of the Code File:The <code>pytest.ini</code> file is crucial for setting up the environment for running tests<br>- It includes several key sections:1<br>- </strong>Test PathsSpecifies where to find test files, including directories.2<br>- <strong>Python FilesLists all Python files in the project.3<br>- </strong>Python ClassesDefines classes that will be tested.4<br>- <strong>Python FunctionsLists functions within each class.5<br>- </strong>Asyncio ModeDetermines whether tests should run asynchronously (e.g., for parallel execution).6<br>- <strong>Add OptionsAllows users to customize test output and verbosity.7<br>- </strong>Filter WarningsSpecifies which warnings should be ignored.### Additional Data:-<strong>Project StructureThe project is structured as a directory structure with subdirectories for different modules or packages.-</strong>File PathThe file path is specified in the <code>pytest.ini</code> file, pointing to the main Python script (<code>phase1/pytest.py</code>).-**Test PathsThe test paths are defined in the <code>testpaths</code> section, which includes directories and files.### Summary:The <code>pytest.ini</code> file provides a comprehensive setup for running tests across the project<br>- It helps in organizing test configurations, ensuring that all tests are executed correctly and efficiently.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/requirements.txt'>requirements.txt</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The project is an open-source Python application that uses FastAPI, Uvicorn, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, and other libraries to build a web API for financial data processing<br>- The code includes core functionalities such as handling HTTP requests, database operations, data connectors, and data processing<br>- It also includes utilities like <code>python-dotenv</code>, <code>cachetools</code>, <code>structlog</code>, <code>pytest</code>, <code>pytest-asyncio</code>, <code>pytest-cov</code>, <code>pytest-mock</code>, and <code>httpx</code><br>- The project is designed to be modular and easy to maintain, with a focus on performance and scalability.</td>
				</tr>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'>Project Structure: The project is structured as a directory structure with subdirectories for different components such as services, routes, and templates.-File Path: The file path provided in the question points to the <code>uvicorn.out</code> file, which is typically used by the FastAPI development server to monitor changes in the application's codebase.This summary provides a concise overview of the main purpose and use of the code file, including its dependencies, functionality, and any additional data about the project.</td>
				</tr>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The README file provides a comprehensive overview of the entire codebase architecture, including the projects structure, dependencies, and commands used to manage its development and testing processes<br>- It covers the installation, development, testing, linting, formatting, running the API server, building Docker images, starting and stopping containers, cleaning up generated files, and providing additional instructions for developers.The README is structured with a clear hierarchy of sections, each covering a specific aspect of the project's architecture<br>- The <code>help</code> command provides a list of available commands, which can be used to run different tasks such as installing dependencies, developing, testing, linting, formatting, running the API server, building Docker images, starting and stopping containers, cleaning up generated files, and providing additional instructions for developers.The README is designed to be easy to read and understand, with clear headings and subheadings that provide a concise overview of each section<br>- The <code>#</code> symbol at the beginning of each line serves as a header, which helps in organizing the content and making it easier to navigate through the documentation.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/docker-compose.yml'>docker-compose.yml</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The provided code file is responsible for setting up a PostgreSQL database and an API server for financial data ingestion using Alpacas API<br>- It includes services for the database, API, and ingestion components<br>- The <code>version</code> field specifies the Docker Compose version used in the project<br>- The <code>services</code> section defines the dependencies between different services, including the database, API, and ingestion<br>- The <code>ports</code> field maps the container ports to host ports<br>- The <code>environment</code> fields set up environment variables for PostgreSQL, Alpaca API keys, and other necessary configurations<br>- The <code>depends_on</code> field ensures that the database service is healthy before starting the API server<br>- The <code>volumes</code> section mounts directories between the containers.</td>
				</tr>
				<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/keys.env.example'>keys.env.example</a></b></td>
					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- API Key ManagementThe file provides credentials for accessing different APIs (Finnhub, Alpaca, PostgreSQL) to interact with the respective services.-<strong>Environment VariablesIt includes variables like API keys and secret keys, which are used in various parts of the application to authenticate requests to these services.### Use:1<br>- </strong>API Key ManagementThe file is crucial for securely accessing financial data through these APIs<br>- By providing the necessary credentials, developers can ensure that their applications have access to the required resources without exposing sensitive information.2<br>- <strong>Environment VariablesThese variables are used in various parts of the application to configure settings such as database connection details and logging levels<br>- This allows developers to easily switch between different environments or configurations without modifying the code.### Additional Data:-</strong>Project StructureThe file is structured as a directory structure, with <code>phase1</code> representing the project's main directory.-<strong>File PathThe file is located at <code>./phase1/keys.env.example</code>.-</strong>ContentThe content of the file includes API keys and environment variables for various financial trading applications.This summary provides a concise overview of the purpose and use of the <code>phase1/keys.env.example</code> file, focusing on its key components and their significance in setting up the environment for various financial trading applications.</td>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>phase1/debug_db.py</code> file is a utility script designed to check the database for the presence of specific symbols and count the number of bars associated with each symbol<br>- It uses SQLAlchemy, an ORM (Object-Relational Mapping) library, to interact with the database<br>- The script initializes the database, retrieves session information, counts the number of bars for each symbol, and prints the results.This file is crucial for maintaining a clean and organized codebase, as it provides a simple way to check the database for specific data and perform operations on it.</td>

						<blockquote>

							<table style='width: 100%; border-collapse: collapse;'>
							<thead>
								<tr style='background-color: #f8f9fa;'>
									<th style='width: 30%; text-align: left; padding: 8px;'>File Name</th>

								</tr>
							</thead>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/bar_engine/engine.py'>engine.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>Forming BarsMaintains a dictionary of bar indices for each symbol, allowing for efficient retrieval and updating.-<strong>Bar Index CalculatorsProvides methods to calculate bar indices and intervals based on timestamps.-</strong>Update CallbacksRegisters callbacks for various events (e.g., form, confirm) that trigger updates.-<strong>Persist CallbackAllows persisting confirmed bars to a database or file.-</strong>Timeframe BoundariesChecks for bars that should be confirmed based on current time.<strong>Additional Notes:</strong>-The code is designed to be modular and reusable, allowing for easy integration with other components in the platform.-It uses asynchronous programming to handle multiple concurrent requests efficiently.-The <code>MultiSymbolBarEngine</code> class provides a unified interface for managing multiple symbols, making it easier to integrate into larger trading platforms.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/bar_engine/session.py'>session.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This calendar handles regular trading hours (9:30 AM-4:00 PM ET) and extended hours (pre-market: 4:00 AM-9:30 AM ET, after-hours: 4:00 PM-8:00 PM ET)<br>- It includes methods to check market open status, get session boundaries, and calculate bar intervals.-**AlwaysOpenCalendarThis calendar is a simple implementation that always returns <code>True</code> for market open status<br>- It provides methods to get session bounds and calculate bar intervals.This class can be extended with additional functionality or integration with other systems as needed.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/bar_engine/lifecycle_manager.py'>lifecycle_manager.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Summary of the Code File**The <code>BarLifecycleManager</code> class is designed to manage bar lifecycle transitions and confirmations based on MarketClock data<br>- It includes functionalities such as creating, updating, confirming, and persisting forming bars<br>- The manager handles various configurations like confirmation delay, emit empty bars, auto-confirm on new bars, and boundary check interval<br>- It also provides methods for checking boundaries and managing forming bars, ensuring accurate and efficient lifecycle management.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/bar_engine/bar_index.py'>bar_index.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a class <code>BarIndexCalculator</code> that calculates deterministic bar indices for a specified symbol and timeframe<br>- It includes methods to calculate the index for a given timestamp, retrieve all timeframes indices, determine which timeframes have a boundary at or before a specific timestamp, and more.The class is designed to be flexible and can handle multiple timeframes by adding more timeframes to the <code>timeframes</code> list<br>- The <code>set_epoch</code> method sets the reference epoch for bar_index calculations, ensuring consistency across different sessions<br>- The <code>get_calculator</code> method retrieves a specific timeframe's BarIndexCalculator instance based on the provided timeframe string.The class also includes methods to calculate indices for all timeframes at a given timestamp and determine which timeframes have a boundary at or before this timestamp<br>- This is useful for updating or confirming bar information in real-time applications.</td>
								</tr>
							</table>
						</blockquote>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/ingestion/normalizer.py'>normalizer.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>NormalizationConverts source-specific tick formats to canonical schema.-<strong>DeduplicationEnsures that each tick is unique based on its source, timestamp, price, and size.-</strong>MonotonicityMaintains the order of ticks per source for efficient processing.-<strong>Callback RegistrationAllows registering callbacks for normalized ticks.-</strong>Buffer ManagementProvides a mechanism to buffer ticks for batch processing.<strong>Additional Information:</strong>-The code uses <code>asyncio</code> for asynchronous operations, which is essential for handling high-throughput scenarios.-The class includes methods for setting and flushing the buffer, as well as managing periodic flush tasks.-The <code>TickBuffer</code> class provides a simple way to manage a buffer of ticks, ensuring efficient processing.This implementation is designed to be flexible and scalable, making it suitable for various applications requiring real-time data normalization.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/ingestion/main.py'>main.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>Data ConnectorsMock, Finnhub, Alpaca, YFinance.-<strong>Tick NormalizerProcesses incoming ticks to normalize data.-</strong>Bar EngineAggregates and stores bar data.-<strong>PersistenceStores bar states in a tiered store for efficient retrieval.-</strong>WebSocket BroadcastingSends updates to the WebSocket server.<strong>Dependencies:</strong>-<code>structlog</code> for logging.-<code>asyncio</code> for asynchronous operations.-<code>signal</code> for handling signals.-<code>os</code> for environment variables.-<code>get_settings</code> function to retrieve configuration settings.<strong>Usage Example:</strong>``<code>python# Initialize and start ingestion serviceservice = IngestionService(mode=live, symbols=[AAPL,MSFT])await service.start()</code>`<code><strong>Running Mock Data Replay:</strong></code>`<code>python# Run mock data replay from CSV filestats = await service.run_mock_replay(path/to/your/csv/file.csv)print(stats)</code>``This summary provides a concise overview of the codes functionality and dependencies.</td>
								</tr>
							</table>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/ingestion/connectors/base.py'>base.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>BaseConnector</code> class provides an abstract interface for data connectors<br>- It includes methods to establish a connection, disconnect, subscribe to tick updates, unsubscribe from tick updates, fetch historical tick data, and register and unregister callbacks<br>- The class also includes a method to emit ticks to all registered callbacks.This code structure is designed to be modular and easy to extend<br>- The <code>BaseConnector</code> class provides a basic framework for interacting with different types of data sources, such as stock prices or financial instruments.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>DependenciesRequires <code>websockets</code> for WebSocket connection and <code>httpx</code> for HTTP requests.-<strong>API KeyUses an environment variable to store the Finnhub API key.-</strong>Error HandlingLogs errors and retries if necessary.-**Candle Data ConversionConverts timestamps from milliseconds to seconds for better data processing.This class provides a robust solution for interacting with the Finnhub API, offering flexibility in handling both live and aggregated data.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- AuthenticationThe connector authenticates with the Alpaca API using a key and secret.2<br>- <strong>SubscriptionIt subscribes to any existing symbols, allowing real-time updates on market events.3<br>- </strong>Historical Data StreamingIt retrieves historical quotes and trades data for specified symbols.4<br>- <strong>Error HandlingIt includes basic error handling for connection issues, message parsing errors, and connection failures.</strong>Dependencies:<strong>-<code>websockets</code>: A library for WebSocket communication in Python.-<code>structlog</code>: For logging purposes.-<code>get_settings</code>: A utility function to retrieve configuration settings from a file.</strong>Additional Information:**This connector is designed to be flexible and can be extended with additional features or optimizations as needed.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>API AuthenticationUses environment variables for API credentials.-<strong>Real-Time UpdatesPolls the API for new trades at regular intervals.-</strong>Historical Data FetchingSupports fetching historical trades from Alpaca's REST API, including bars directly from the API.-<strong>Side MappingMaps trade conditions to side if determinable.</strong>Additional Notes:**-The class is designed to be flexible and can be extended with additional functionality as needed.-The code includes basic error handling and logging for better management of network issues.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/ingestion/connectors/yfinance_connector.py'>yfinance_connector.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a basic implementation of a yfinance connector that fetches historical data from the stock market<br>- It includes methods to connect to yfinance, subscribe to symbols, and retrieve historical ticks or bars<br>- The code is designed to be efficient and handle asynchronous operations.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/ingestion/connectors/mock.py'>mock.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>MockConnector</code> class is designed to simulate a data source that can be used for testing purposes, including reading tick data from CSV files<br>- It subscribes to specific symbols and provides methods to load ticks into memory buffers, replay them through callbacks, inject new ticks, and clear the buffer.<strong>Additional Information:</strong>-<strong>Context Details:</strong> The project is structured as a directory structure with a file named <code>phase1/services/ingestion/connectors/mock.py</code>.-<strong>File Content:</strong> This file contains a class <code>MockConnector</code> that implements basic functionality for simulating data sources.-<strong>Initialization:</strong> The connector subscribes to specified symbols and initializes the buffer.-<strong>Reading Data:</strong> It reads ticks from a CSV file, filtering by symbol and time range.-<strong>Replaying Data:</strong> It simulates real-time delays between ticks.-<strong>Injecting Ticks:</strong> It allows injecting new ticks into the buffer for testing purposes.-<strong>Clearing Buffer:</strong> It clears the internal buffer to prepare for future replay.This class is useful for testing data sources and ensuring that they can handle a variety of scenarios, including deterministic behavior and real-time delays.</td>

					<!-- autopilot Submodule -->
					<details>
						<summary><b>autopilot</b></summary>
						<blockquote>
							<div class='directory-path' style='padding: 8px 0; color: #666;'>
								<code><b>⦿ phase1.services.autopilot</b></code>
							<table style='width: 100%; border-collapse: collapse;'>
							<thead>
								<tr style='background-color: #f8f9fa;'>
									<th style='width: 30%; text-align: left; padding: 8px;'>File Name</th>
									<th style='text-align: left; padding: 8px;'>Summary</th>
								</tr>
							</thead>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/news_sentiment.py'>news_sentiment.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Data IntegrationThe script fetches news from various sources (e.g., Finnhub, Yahoo Finance) and stores them in a structured format.2<br>- <strong>Sentiment AnalysisIt uses machine learning algorithms to analyze the sentiment of each news item based on predefined categories and buckets.3<br>- </strong>News Impact ScoringIt calculates the impact score for each news item based on factors like market volume, company performance, and industry trends.4<br>- <strong>Recency WeightingThe script weights the news items by their recency (breaking vs<br>- recent) to prioritize them more frequently in the feed.5<br>- </strong>LoggingThe script logs the progress of the sentiment analysis process, including the start time, end time, and any errors encountered.6<br>- <strong>Error HandlingThe script includes basic error handling for network requests and API responses.7<br>- </strong>DependenciesIt uses <code>httpx</code> to interact with the Finnhub API, which is a popular choice for making HTTP requests in Python.8<br>- **Data ClassesThe code defines data classes for news categories, sentiment buckets, and recency buckets, which are used to store and manage the data.This script provides a robust framework for handling news sentiment analysis, leveraging the power of machine learning and data processing techniques.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/execution_simulator.py'>execution_simulator.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file is a Python implementation of a deterministic paper execution simulator<br>- It includes methods to simulate fill attempts, handle partial fill incidents, and retrieve metrics about the simulations performance<br>- The simulator uses deterministic randomness to ensure reproducibility and provides detailed metrics on fill outcomes, including average slippage, time-to-fill buckets, and partial fill incidents.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/research_reports.py'>research_reports.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Provides a summary of the total return, win rate, and average profit for each trade.2<br>- <strong>Template AttributionAnalyzes template performance by showing the number of winners and their respective profits.3<br>- </strong>Regime AttributionIdentifies which regimes are most likely to perform well based on the trade data.4<br>- <strong>Parameter SweepGenerates a heatmap of DTE × Delta distributions, allowing for detailed analysis of trading strategies.5<br>- </strong>Fragility AnalysisCalculates the expected return under stress and provides insights into potential failure modes.### Usage1<br>- <strong>InstallationEnsure you have Python installed on your system.2<br>- </strong>Run ScriptExecute the script using a Python interpreter.3<br>- <strong>Export ReportUse the <code>to_json</code> method to export the report as JSON, which can be saved or shared with others.### Additional Information-</strong>Context DetailsThe project is structured around a single file (<code>phase1/services/autopilot/research_reports.py</code>) and includes data classes for trade records, portfolio metrics, template attribution, regime attribution, parameter sweep cells, and fragility results.-<strong>Data ClassesEach class has attributes that encapsulate the relevant information about each trade or report element.-</strong>Template BreakdownThe <code>template_breakdown</code> attribute provides a breakdown of which templates are most likely to perform well based on the trade data.-**Performance MetricsThe script calculates metrics such as total return, win rate, and average profit for each trade.This script is designed to be flexible and can be easily extended or modified to include additional features or reporting tools.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/alpaca_broker.py'>alpaca_broker.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Summary of the Code File**The <code>AlpacaOptionsBroker</code> class is designed to submit options trades to Alpacas paper trading API<br>- It handles both internal simulation and external submission, ensuring visibility in the dashboard<br>- The class includes methods to generate OCC standard option symbols, submit orders, retrieve recent activities, and close positions<br>- The code is structured with clear documentation and error handling for better maintainability and functionality.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/universe.py'>universe.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Liquidity FilterThe class supports different liquidity requirements (min underlying volume, max option spread percentage, min open interest) based on predefined configurations.-<strong>Symbol InitializationThe <code>initialize</code> method initializes the universe with a list of allowed symbols and optionally a liquidity filter.-</strong>Data UpdateThe <code>update_symbol_data</code> method updates data for a symbol, including last price, average volume, option spread percentage, and open interest<br>- It recalculates the liquidity score.-<strong>Tradeable SymbolsThe <code>get_tradeable_symbols</code> method filters out symbols that are not tradeable based on their liquidity score.-</strong>Earnings Blackout CheckThe <code>is_in_earnings_blackout</code> method checks if a symbol is in an earnings blackout period by comparing its next earnings date with the current date.-<strong>Symbol RetrievalThe <code>get_symbols_with_upcoming_earnings</code> method retrieves symbols that have upcoming earnings within a specified number of days.-</strong>Dictionary ConversionThe <code>to_dict</code> method converts the universe to a dictionary for easy serialization and storage.This class provides a robust framework for managing trading data, ensuring efficient liquidity management and symbol availability.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/paper_broker.py'>paper_broker.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Order SubmissionUsers can submit paper orders by providing trade candidate details.2<br>- <strong>ExecutionOrders are executed based on predefined conditions, including market prices and slippage.3<br>- </strong>CancelationOrders can be cancelled to prevent further execution.4<br>- <strong>MetricsProvides fill quality metrics such as total orders, filled orders, rejected orders, and cancelled orders.</strong>Additional Information:<strong>-</strong>Simulation ParametersThe module uses a base fill probability of 0.95, with a range of 0-2% slippage for market orders and 0-2% commission for limit orders.-<strong>CommissionsEach filled order includes a commission based on the number of contracts executed.-</strong>Order SubmissionUsers can submit orders using either a candidate or a symbol, providing additional context if needed.-**Execution ProcessThe module simulates the execution process by applying slippage and calculating fill prices for each leg.This module is useful for testing and understanding the behavior of paper trading systems.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/reporting.py'>reporting.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>ReportingModule</code> class in the <code>phase1/services/autopilot/reporting.py</code> file is designed to generate daily reports and attribution analysis for a P&L (Performance and Loss Analysis) system<br>- This module utilizes data classes to encapsulate the attributes of each report, including template, trade count, win count, loss count, gross pnl, commission, net pnl, average pnl per trade, win rate, average win, average loss, and profit factor.The <code>ReportingModule</code> class is structured as a dataclass with fields for each attribute<br>- The <code>to_dict()</code> method is used to convert the data class into a dictionary that can be easily serialized or transmitted.This module provides a basic framework for generating reports based on P&L data, which can be useful for various applications such as financial analysis, reporting systems, and more.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/service.py'>service.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>AutopilotService</code> class is a singleton that manages the lifecycle of an autopilot system<br>- It initializes with data providers and config, creates a runloop, and starts a background loop to monitor and execute scheduled cycles<br>- The service can be stopped by canceling the background task or manually stopping the runloop.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/monitoring.py'>monitoring.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Continuous Monitoring-The code uses <code>httpx</code> for making HTTP requests to Alpacas API.-It fetches position data from the API using a specific endpoint (e.g., <code>/positions/{position_id}</code>).-It processes the response to extract relevant information about positions, such as their status and target quantity.2<br>- <strong>Automatic Exit Management-The code uses <code>PositionManager</code> to handle position management.-It checks for exit conditions based on predefined criteria (e.g., profit targets, stop losses, time stops).-If an exit condition is met, it triggers the corresponding action (e.g., closing a position).3<br>- </strong>Trade Lifecycle Issues-The code handles various trade lifecycle issues such as:-Profit target-Stop loss-Time stop-DTE threshold-Earnings close-Manual exit-Risk limit-News shock-Maximum hold time4<br>- <strong>Configuration and Logging-The code uses <code>AutopilotConfig</code> to load configuration settings.-It logs relevant information using Python's built-in logging module.### Additional Data:-</strong>DependenciesThe project depends on <code>httpx</code>, which is a popular HTTP client for making API requests in Python<br>- It is included in the project's requirements.-<strong>Environment VariablesThe code uses environment variables to store configuration settings, such as API keys and other sensitive information.-</strong>Logging ConfigurationThe logging configuration includes file paths and log levels.This code provides a robust framework for monitoring and managing positions within an Alpaca trading environment<br>- It is designed to be flexible and easy to maintain, with clear documentation and support for future enhancements.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/hybrid_selector.py'>hybrid_selector.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The selector uses Groqs fast ranking capabilities to quickly identify top-K candidates.2<br>- <strong>Validation and ExplanationIt validates the selected candidates against predefined criteria (e.g., high POP, good risk/reward ratio, liquidity score) and provides detailed explanations.3<br>- </strong>Fallback MechanismIf either LLM provider fails, it falls back to deterministic selection using a fallback mechanism.### UsageTo use the <strong>Hybrid Selector</strong>, you can create an instance with optional providers:``<code>pythonfrom ..llm.providers import create_groq_provider, create_gemini_provider# Create a hybrid selector with Groq and Gemini providershybrid_selector = create_hybrid_selector(groq_provider=create_groq_provider(), gemini_provider=create_gemini_provider())</code>``### Additional Information-<strong>EnvironmentThe code is designed to work in environments where both Groq and Gemini are available.-</strong>PerformanceIt leverages Groq's fast ranking capabilities, which can be particularly useful for large datasets.-<strong>ValidationIt validates the selected candidates against predefined criteria, ensuring accuracy and reliability.-</strong>Fallback MechanismIf either LLM provider fails, it provides a fallback mechanism to maintain user experience.This setup is designed to be flexible and adaptable to different use cases, making it suitable for various industries and applications.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/ledger.py'>ledger.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a basic implementation of a trade ledger system using Python and the <code>dataclasses</code> module<br>- The <code>TradeLedger</code> class manages entries, validation, and summary operations<br>- It also includes methods to persist the ledger to disk and load it from disk<br>- The global instance is managed through the <code>get_ledger</code> function, which returns the current instance of the ledger<br>- The <code>reset_ledger</code> function can be used for testing purposes by resetting the ledger to its initial state.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/position_manager.py'>position_manager.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Position Manager Module in the phase1/services/autopilot directory manages options position ledger with Greeks tracking<br>- It includes classes for <code>TradeCandidate</code>, <code>OptionLeg</code>, and <code>PaperOrder</code><br>- The module provides methods to add, update, and delete positions, as well as to calculate Greeks exposure based on the current market conditions.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/validator.py'>validator.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Risk Guardrails-<strong>No Trade ExceededEnsures that no trade can exceed the defined risk limit.-</strong>Total Risk ExceededPrevents a trade from exceeding the total risk limit.-<strong>Daily Loss ExceededLimits the daily loss of a trade to prevent it from exceeding the specified threshold.-</strong>Position Limit ExceededPrevents a trade from exceeding the position limit, which is crucial for maintaining market liquidity.2<br>- <strong>Symbol Concentration and Cluster Concentration-Ensures that the trading volume does not exceed the symbol concentration or cluster concentration limits.-This helps in managing risk by limiting the number of trades per symbol or cluster.3<br>- </strong>Earnings Blackout-Prevents a trade from exceeding the earnings blackout threshold, which is crucial for maintaining market liquidity and profitability.4<br>- <strong>Liquidity Too Low-Ensures that the trading volume does not exceed the liquidity threshold.-This helps in managing risk by limiting the number of trades per symbol or cluster.5<br>- </strong>Spread Too Wide-Limits the spread of a trade to prevent it from exceeding the specified threshold.-This helps in managing risk by ensuring that the spread is within acceptable limits.6<br>- <strong>DTE Out of Range-Ensures that the trading date does not exceed the specified DTE range.-This helps in managing risk by limiting the number of trades per symbol or cluster.7<br>- </strong>Invalid Legs-Prevents a trade from having an invalid leg, which could lead to incorrect calculations or market behavior.-This helps in maintaining the integrity of the trading process.8<br>- <strong>Template Not Allowed-Ensures that only templates are allowed for validation.-This helps in maintaining the integrity of the trading process and preventing unauthorized modifications.9<br>- </strong>Underlying Not Allowed-Prevents a trade from having an underlying asset, which could lead to incorrect calculations or market behavior.-This helps in maintaining the integrity of the trading process and preventing unauthorized modifications.10<br>- <strong>IV Out Of Range-Limits the IV (Internal Value) of a trade to prevent it from exceeding the specified threshold.-This helps in managing risk by ensuring that the IV is within acceptable limits.### Additional Data:-</strong>Context DetailsThe project is designed for a trading platform, specifically focusing on automated trading strategies<br>- It uses data classes and enums to represent different types of trades and their statuses.-<strong>File PathThe file is located in the <code>phase1/services/autopilot</code> directory.-</strong>FunctionalityThe code provides a comprehensive set of validation logic that ensures trade integrity and market performance.### Summary:The <code>validator.py</code> file serves as a robust tool for validating trades, ensuring that no trade exceeds predefined thresholds<br>- It includes features such as risk guardrails, symbol concentration, cluster concentration, earnings blackout, liquidity too low, spread too wide, DTE out of range, invalid legs, template not allowed, and underlying not allowed<br>- The code is designed to</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/monitor.py'>monitor.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>PositionMonitor</code> class is designed to monitor positions and generate exit signals based on predefined rules<br>- It checks various conditions such as profit target, loss limit, time stop, earnings proximity, and portfolio-level risk metrics<br>- The class also handles alerts by creating and managing them, ensuring that only active alerts are executed.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/unified_cycle.py'>unified_cycle.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Unified Autopilot Cycle is a single workflow that handles data refresh, portfolio state refresh, monitoring pass, candidate generation, selection and validation, execution via Alpaca, decision trace persistence, and UI update triggers<br>- This cycle is designed to ensure the systems performance and reliability by continuously updating its state based on external inputs and decisions.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/selector.py'>selector.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Candidate Selection StrategyThe class defines an abstract base class <code>CandidateSelector</code> with methods for selecting candidates based on various criteria.2<br>- <strong>Deterministic SelectionThe <code>deterministic_selection</code> method is implemented to select candidates using a deterministic selection strategy, which means that each candidate has the same probability of being selected.3<br>- </strong>LLM-Based SelectionThe <code>llm_based_selection</code> method is implemented to select candidates using an LLM-based selection strategy, which involves generating text responses based on the user's input and providing recommendations.4<br>- <strong>LoggingThe class includes logging functionality to track the execution process and any errors that occur during selection.5<br>- </strong>Fallback MechanismThe <code>fallback_used</code> attribute indicates whether a fallback mechanism was used during selection, which can be useful for scenarios where the user might not have provided enough information or if there are no candidates available.### Additional Data About the ProjectThe project structure is described in the context details section<br>- This includes the project's directory structure, file paths, and any additional files or directories that may be relevant to the implementation of the <code>CandidateSelector</code> class.This code file serves as a foundation for implementing different selection strategies in the autopilot system, providing a robust solution to candidate ranking and selection.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/config.py'>config.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Budget and Risk LimitsDefines the maximum risk per trade and total risk, as well as the daily loss threshold.-<strong>Universe and Strategy WhitelistsLists supported universes and allowed strategy templates.-</strong>Forecast Influence SettingsConfigures the forecast influence settings for decisions.-<strong>LLM EnablementEnables or disables LLM ranking based on user preference.-</strong>Schedule (Times)Defines the times at which the system should execute paper trades, with options to specify blackout days before and auto-close events.### Configuration Storage:The file uses a JSON file (<code>autopilot_config.json</code>) to store configuration settings<br>- This allows for easy management of different configurations across different environments or projects.### Additional Information:-<strong>Environment VariablesThe script includes environment variables for LLM mode, Groq API key, and Gemini API key.-</strong>Configuration ModesThe script maps string values to enum constants for better readability.-**Error HandlingIt includes basic error handling for missing required keys in the configuration file.This setup provides a structured way to manage and configure various settings related to paper-only AI options autopilot, ensuring that configurations are easily accessible and updated.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/runloop.py'>runloop.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>RunloopModule</code> in the provided code is designed to orchestrate a complete autopilot cycle, which involves scanning for candidates, selecting them based on predefined criteria, executing trades, monitoring performance, and reporting results<br>- The module uses various components such as <code>UniverseManager</code>, <code>FeatureEngine</code>, <code>CandidateGenerator</code>, <code>TradeCandidate</code>, <code>CandidateSelector</code>, <code>DeterministicRanker</code>, <code>SelectionResult</code>, <code>create_selector</code>, <code>PaperBroker</code>, <code>PaperOrder</code>, <code>OrderStatus</code>, <code>PositionManager</code>, <code>PortfolioState</code>, <code>Monitor</code>, <code>PositionMonitor</code>, and <code>Reporting</code> to handle the entire cycle<br>- The module also includes logging, error handling, and reporting functionalities.The code is structured with a class <code>RunloopState</code> that defines the possible states of the autopilot runloop, including <code>IDLE</code>, <code>RUNNING</code>, <code>PAUSED</code>, and <code>ERROR</code><br>- Each state has a corresponding enum value for clarity and to differentiate between different states<br>- The <code>CycleResult</code> class encapsulates the results of each cycle, providing details such as the cycle ID.The code uses various data classes and functions from the <code>dataclasses</code> module to define and manage the components of the autopilot runloop<br>- It also utilizes other modules like <code>UniverseManager</code>, <code>FeatureEngine</code>, <code>CandidateGenerator</code>, <code>TradeCandidate</code>, <code>CandidateSelector</code>, <code>DeterministicRanker</code>, <code>SelectionResult</code>, <code>create_selector</code>, <code>PaperBroker</code>, <code>PaperOrder</code>, <code>OrderStatus</code>, <code>PositionManager</code>, <code>PortfolioState</code>, <code>Monitor</code>, and <code>Reporting</code> to handle the specific tasks of each component.Overall, the <code>RunloopModule</code> is designed to be modular, scalable, and maintainable<br>- It uses various components from different modules to achieve its goals while providing clear documentation and error handling.</td>
								</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/v1_templates.py'>v1_templates.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides comprehensive documentation on the V1 strategy templates defined in the <code>autopilot/v1_templates.py</code> file of an open-source project<br>- It includes details about template parameters, width bounds by underlying price, credit/Debit constraints, suitability regimes, and IV environments<br>- The code also includes validation functions to ensure that candidate strategies meet specific criteria, such as DTE, delta, width, credit/Debit ratio, and regulatory requirements.</td>
								</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/trade_stream.py'>trade_stream.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a basic implementation of an Alpaca Trade Updates WebSocket client using the <code>websockets</code> library<br>- It includes methods to connect to the websocket, handle trade updates, and manage callbacks for different event types<br>- The code is designed to be modular and easy to extend with additional features or optimizations.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/data_fetcher.py'>data_fetcher.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>MarketDataProvider</code> class combines the capabilities of the <code>TradierDataFetcher</code> and <code>YFinanceDataFetcher</code> to provide a comprehensive set of market data services<br>- It fetches stock prices, historical closing prices, and options chains from both Tradier and yFinance, ensuring that the data is up-to-date and accurate for various trading scenarios<br>- The class uses caching mechanisms to store frequently accessed data, reducing the load on the API and improving performance.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/state_manager.py'>state_manager.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>StateManager</code> class manages the persistence of autopilot state to JSON files<br>- It saves and loads the current state of position managers and paper brokers, ensuring that the data is consistent across different instances of the application<br>- The class uses helper functions to convert between dictionaries and objects, which are essential for handling complex data structures.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/features.py'>features.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- A robust tool for computing market features used for strategy selection<br>- It computes trends, volatility regimes, IV rank and percentile, liquidity scores, and forecasts based on historical data.<strong>Market Regime AssessmentThe <code>get_market_regime</code> method provides a current assessment of the market regime based on spy prices and VIX level.</strong>Calibration AdjustmentThe <code>get_calibration_adjustment</code> method calculates a calibration adjustment factor for a symbol<br>- This is useful for adjusting forecast influence to better reflect actual returns within specific bands.This implementation is designed to be flexible and can be extended with additional features or optimizations as needed.</td>
								</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/autopilot/candidates.py'>candidates.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Candidate Status EnumDefines the possible statuses for candidate objects, such as <code>PENDING</code>, <code>SELECTED</code>, <code>REJECTED</code>, and <code>EXECUTED</code>.2<br>- <strong>OptionLeg ClassRepresents a single option leg within a strategy<br>- It includes fields for option type, strike price, expiry date, side (buy or sell), quantity, premium, delta, gamma, theta, vega, and other relevant attributes.3<br>- </strong>Data ClassesUtilizes <code>dataclass</code> from the <code>dataclasses</code> module to define classes with specific fields and methods<br>- This helps in organizing and managing data more efficiently.4<br>- <strong>UniverseManager ClassManages the universe of symbols, allowing for easy access to symbol details and trading capabilities.5<br>- </strong>SymbolFeatures ClassProvides a set of features related to each symbol, such as volatility, trend direction, and market conditions.6<br>- <strong>TrendDirection EnumDefines the possible trends for symbols, such as bullish or bearish.7<br>- </strong>VolatilityRegime EnumDefines the different volatility regimes for symbols, such as bullish, bearish, and neutral.8<br>- <strong>LoggerUses Python's <code>logging</code> module to log relevant information about candidate generation and execution processes.### Additional Data:-</strong>AutopilotConfig ClassContains configuration settings for the autopilot system, including market conditions, strategy templates, and other relevant parameters.-**StrategyTemplate ClassDefines a template for trading strategies, including option types, strike prices, expiry dates, side (buy or sell), quantity, premium, delta, gamma, theta, vega, and other relevant attributes.This code structure is designed to be modular and easy to maintain, with clear separation of concerns between data management, strategy generation, and execution.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Handles the lifecycle of WebSocket connections, allowing for automatic subscription and disconnection.-<strong>Symbol/Timeframe SubscriptionsAllows subscribing to specific symbols and timeframes.-</strong>Broadcasting Bar UpdatesSends messages to all subscribed connections about new bars.-<strong>Error HandlingProvides robust error handling for connection issues, message parsing errors, and other network-related issues.### ArchitectureThe code is structured with a class <code>ConnectionManager</code> that manages WebSocket connections and subscriptions<br>- It uses a lock to ensure thread safety when accessing the connection dictionary.### Callback Functions-</strong>on_bar_updateCalled whenever a new bar update is received.-<strong>on_bar_confirmedCalled when a bar confirmation is received.### Additional Information1<br>- </strong>DependenciesThe project depends on <code>fastapi</code> for handling HTTP requests and <code>structlog</code> for logging.2<br>- <strong>PersistenceThe code uses a tiered store to cache historical bar data, which can improve performance by reducing the number of database queries.3<br>- </strong>Error HandlingThe code includes basic error handling for connection issues, message parsing errors, and network-related issues.This architecture is designed to be modular and scalable, making it easy to add new features or integrate with other services in the future.</td>
								</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This function checks the health status of the Alpaca account using the <code>APCA_API_BASE_URL</code> and <code>APCA_API_KEY_ID</code><br>- It returns an object containing API reachability, account status, trading capabilities, and account balances.2<br>- <strong>Recent ActivityThis function retrieves recent activity from the Alpaca account using the <code>APCA_API_BASE_URL</code> and <code>APCA_API_KEY_ID</code><br>- It includes orders, positions, and account activities for the last 24 hours.3<br>- </strong>Broker StatusThis function checks the connection status of the broker and returns a summary of the broker's status, including connected status, last check time, account ID, cash balance, equity, buying power, and positions synced status.4<br>- <strong>Last Run VerificationThis function verifies the last autopilot run against Alpaca records using the <code>APCA_API_BASE_URL</code> and <code>APCA_API_KEY_ID</code><br>- It compares internal ledger entries with PLACED/FILLED status, against Alpaca order records, and reports any discrepancies.5<br>- </strong>Run RouteThis function verifies a specific autopilot run by ID using the <code>APCA_API_BASE_URL</code> and <code>APCA_API_KEY_ID</code><br>- It checks for paper trade status and returns results in a summary format.<strong>Additional Instructions</strong>-<strong>Context DetailsThe code file is designed to be used within an open-source project, which requires understanding of Alpaca's API and its requirements<br>- The context includes the necessary environment variables (<code>APCA_API_BASE_URL</code>, <code>APCA_API_KEY_ID</code>) for authentication.-</strong>Error HandlingThe code includes basic error handling to manage potential issues such as network errors or invalid API responses.-<strong>LoggingDetailed logging is used to track the execution process and any discrepancies found during verification.-</strong>Data ValidationThe code ensures that all required data fields are present in the request body, including internal ledger entries, Alpaca order records, and any other relevant information.This file provides a robust solution for verifying trades using Alpacas API, ensuring compliance with the project's requirements.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>versions.py</code> file in the <code>phase1/services/api/routes/versions.py</code> module provides a RESTful API endpoint for managing versioning strategies<br>- It includes endpoints to list all versions, get specific versions by ID, create new versions, compute diffs between two versions, and rollback to a specific version.This code is designed to be modular and scalable, allowing for easy integration with other services or applications<br>- The <code>VersionCreate</code> model defines the structure of a version creation request, while the <code>VersionResponse</code>, <code>DiffResponse</code>, and <code>RollbackVersion</code> models define the response formats for each endpoint.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The purpose of the <code>packages</code> API routes is to manage and retrieve information about installed packages within a marketplace<br>- The routes provide endpoints for listing all packages, retrieving details for specific packages, installing a package from a bundle, uninstalling a package, enabling or disabling a package, and getting permissions for a package.Additional data includes validation using Pydantic for ensuring that the inputs are valid and that dangerous capabilities are not included in the response.</td>

										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/api/routes/clock.py'>clock.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The code file serves as a RESTful API for managing the MarketClock, allowing users to interact with its state, start/stop execution, and adjust its speed<br>- It uses FastAPI for handling HTTP requests and Pydantic for data validation and model definitions.### Use of the Code File:1<br>- <strong>GET <code>/clock_state</code>:</strong> Retrieves the current state of the clock.2<br>- <strong>POST <code>/mode</code>:</strong> Sets the mode of the clock to either <code>LIVE</code> or <code>VIRTUAL</code>.3<br>- <strong>POST <code>/control</code>:</strong> Controls the execution of the clock by setting its mode, starting/running, or stopping.4<br>- <strong>POST <code>/advance</code>:</strong> Advances the virtual clock by a specified number of milliseconds.5<br>- <strong>POST <code>/speed</code>:</strong> Sets the speed multiplier for the virtual clock.### Additional Data:-<strong>FastAPI Version:</strong> The code uses FastAPI version 3.10.2.-<strong>Pydantic Version:</strong> The code uses Pydantic version 6.1.4.-<strong>Database Integration:</strong> The code interacts with a database to store and retrieve clock state data.-<strong>Logging Configuration:</strong> The code includes logging configuration using <code>structlog</code> for detailed logging.This code file provides a basic framework for managing the MarketClock, making it easy to extend and integrate into larger projects.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The purpose of the <code>runs.py</code> file is to manage strategy runs within an orchestrator<br>- It provides endpoints for creating, listing, and managing run instances, including starting, pausing, resuming, stopping, and retrieving logs for each run<br>- The API uses Pydantic models for data validation and response formats.</td>
										</tr>
										<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/api/routes/autopilot.py'>autopilot.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The script allows users to update configuration settings such as paper equity, mode, risk limits, allowed templates, forecast influence, LLM enablement, and more.2<br>- <strong>Run LoopIt provides a simple API for triggering an autopilot cycle with optional force execution.3<br>- </strong>Position ManagementOffers methods to get open positions, close all positions, and retrieve detailed position ledger.4<br>- <strong>LogsProvides structured logs of the system's activity, including trade reports, alerts, and metrics.5<br>- </strong>Kill SwitchAllows users to activate or deactivate the kill switch for runaway behavior.6<br>- <strong>ReportingGenerates daily autopilot report with P&L summary, trading activity, attribution by strategy/symbol, and risk metrics.### Additional Information-</strong>DependenciesThe script relies on <code>fastapi</code> for API endpoints, <code>pydantic</code> for data models, and <code>structlog</code> for logging.-<strong>State ManagementThe script uses a singleton instance of <code>AutopilotRunloop</code> to manage the autopilot's lifecycle.-</strong>Error HandlingThe script includes basic error handling for common issues like invalid configurations or network errors.This code provides a basic framework for managing an autopilot system, which can be expanded with additional features and optimizations as needed.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Performance Report Generation-Supports generating performance reports based on strategy details.-Allows for customizing report parameters like start date, end date, trades, metrics, and alerts.2<br>- <strong>Audit Trail Report Generation-Provides a detailed audit trail of orders and alerts.-Includes customizable start and end dates for the report generation.3<br>- </strong>API Endpoints:<strong>-<code>/reports</code> provides a list of all generated reports.-<code>/reports/{report_id}</code> retrieves a specific report by ID.-<code>/reports/performance</code> generates a performance report.-<code>/reports/audit</code> generates an audit trail report.4<br>- </strong>Error Handling-Includes basic error handling for missing or invalid parameters in the request body.5<br>- <strong>Security Considerations:</strong>-Uses <code>fastapi.security.BasicAuth</code> to authenticate requests with a username and password.-Ensures that only authorized users can access these endpoints.6<br>- <strong>Documentation-Provides detailed documentation for each endpoint, including request/response models, parameters, and error handling.</strong>Additional Data:**-The code uses a hypothetical <code>get_report_generator</code> function to simulate the generation of reports based on provided data.-The project is designed to be modular and scalable, with clear separation of concerns between different functionalities.-The use of <code>fastapi.security.BasicAuth</code> ensures that only authenticated users can access these endpoints.This summary provides a comprehensive overview of the code files purpose, functionality, and security features.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The provided code file <code>phase1/services/api/routes/debug.py</code> serves as a debug endpoint for various functionalities within the application<br>- It includes endpoints to calculate bar indices and session calendars, as well as statistics about the database connection and configuration settings<br>- The code is designed to be self-contained and clear, with appropriate error handling and logging.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/api/routes/patterns.py'>patterns.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Users can use this API to identify trends, anomalies, or patterns in stock prices.2<br>- <strong>Machine LearningFor data-driven applications, the endpoint can be used to predict future market movements based on historical patterns.3<br>- </strong>User InterfaceThe API can be integrated into a web application for users to visualize and analyze chart patterns.### Technical Implementation Details-<strong>API EndpointThe endpoint is defined using FastAPI, which provides a simple and efficient way to handle HTTP requests.-</strong>Parameters-<code>symbol</code>: The ticker symbol for which to detect patterns.-<code>timeframe</code>: The timeframe (e.g., 1D, 5M) over which to analyze the data.-<code>limit</code>: The number of bars to consider for analysis.-<code>confidence_threshold</code>: The minimum confidence required for a pattern to be detected.-<strong>Data RetrievalThe API fetches historical bar data using a repository, which is assumed to be implemented in the <code>BarRepository</code> class.-</strong>Pattern DetectionThe <code>PatternDetector</code> class is used to detect patterns based on various parameters such as ATR values, VWAP, and POC (Pricing of Opportunities).-**Filtering and SortingPatterns are filtered by confidence threshold and sorted by end time for easy visualization.### Additional DataThe project includes a <code>BarRepository</code> class that can be implemented using a database or other data storage solution<br>- This repository will handle the retrieval of bar data from a database, which is necessary to perform pattern detection.This API provides a robust solution for detecting chart patterns in financial markets and can be easily integrated into web applications for further analysis.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/api/routes/incidents.py'>incidents.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>Incidents</code> API routes provide a comprehensive set of endpoints to capture and replay incidents from various run IDs and strategies<br>- This includes methods to list all captured incidents, retrieve specific incident details, start capturing events, stop capturing, replay an incident, and export the incident bundle as JSON.This code is designed to be modular and scalable, allowing for easy integration with other services or libraries<br>- The <code>IncidentSummary</code> model provides a structured way to represent the data returned by each endpoint, making it easier to work with and integrate into larger applications.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides endpoints for retrieving bar data from a stock symbol and timeframe<br>- It includes functions to get the most recent bar, count bars, and retrieve specific bars by index<br>- The code uses Pydantic for data validation and structlog for logging errors.</td>
