											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/api/routes/portfolio.py'>portfolio.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The portfolio API provides endpoints for retrieving the current state of a portfolio, listing all positions, and exporting trade history<br>- It also includes metrics such as equity, cash, and performance<br>- The backend uses in-memory data structures to store the portfolio state and orders, ensuring that the application can handle large volumes of transactions without performance degradation.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/api/routes/automation.py'>automation.py</a></b></td>
											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Users can retrieve the current status of their autopilot, including whether its armed, in which mode, and how much they've spent.2<br>- <strong>Job SubmissionUsers can submit new jobs for automation, such as simple moving averages or custom scripts.3<br>- </strong>Job CancellationUsers can cancel pending or queued jobs to free up resources.4<br>- <strong>Readiness ScoreProvides a readiness score based on backtest results, robustness metrics, and regime alignment.## </strong>Technical Implementation Details<strong>-</strong>FastAPIUsed for API endpoints and routing.-<strong>PydanticFor data validation and model definitions.-</strong>Database IntegrationUtilizes an in-memory state to manage job queues and forecasts.-<strong>Concurrency ControlImplemented using a thread-safe queue system.## </strong>Additional Information<strong>This codebase is designed to be modular, allowing for easy extension and maintenance<br>- It includes features such as role-based access control (RBAC) for different users, logging, and error handling<br>- The architecture also supports integration with external services like yfinance for data fetching.## </strong>Deployment Considerations<strong>-</strong>EnvironmentThe codebase is deployed on a production environment using Docker.-<strong>SecurityImplementing robust security measures such as authentication, authorization, and encryption.-</strong>MonitoringUtilize tools like Prometheus and Grafana to monitor API performance and job status.## <strong>Future Enhancements</strong>-<strong>Integration with External ServicesAdd support for external services like AWS Lambda or Google Cloud Functions for more advanced automation including API specifications, error responses, and usage examples.## </strong>Conclusion**This codebase provides a robust framework for managing and controlling an autopilot strategy using Python, designed to be modular, scalable, and easy to maintain, making it suitable for deployment on a production environment.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>execute</code> method runs a function in a separate process, utilizing resource limits to manage system resources.2<br>- <strong>Validation Strategy CodeThe <code>validate_strategy_code</code> method checks for forbidden imports and dangerous constructs within the strategy code.3<br>- </strong>Running Strategy BarThe <code>run_strategy_bar</code> method executes a strategy's <code>on_bar</code> method in the sandbox.<strong>Security Considerations</strong>-<strong>Resource LimitsThe sandbox uses resource limits to manage system resources, such as CPU time and memory.-</strong>Network BlockingThe sandbox restricts network access through resource limits.-<strong>Restricted ImportsThe sandbox restricts imports based on allowed modules.-</strong>Allowed ModulesThe sandbox allows a list of allowed modules.<strong>Error Handling</strong>-<strong>Timeout ErrorIf the execution exceeds the specified timeout, it raises a <code>TimeoutError</code>.-</strong>Memory ErrorIf the memory usage exceeds the specified limit, it raises a <code>MemoryError</code>.-<strong>Security ErrorIf the strategy violates security policies, it raises a <code>SecurityError</code>.</strong>Diagnostics**The <code>get_diagnostics</code> method provides detailed execution diagnostics, including total errors, warnings, and last 10 errors.This architecture is designed to be modular and scalable, allowing for easy integration with other components of the system.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a comprehensive implementation of a replay controller for managing historical data replay sessions<br>- It includes the necessary classes and methods to load ticks, play/pause/seek, and manage progress<br>- The code is designed to be modular and easy to extend, with clear documentation and comments explaining each part of the system.</td>
								</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/replay/tick_replayer.py'>tick_replayer.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Deterministic Tick ReplayerThe replayer maintains a consistent tick sequence across different runs, ensuring that the same input produces the same output.2<br>- <strong>Clock-Synchronized PlaybackIt uses a virtual clock mode to ensure that the replay process is synchronized with the market clock, allowing for accurate timing and synchronization of ticks.3<br>- </strong>Speed ControlThe replayer can be configured to adjust its speed based on the source's performance or market conditions.4<br>- <strong>Gap HandlingIt skips large time gaps if configured, ensuring that the replay process remains responsive even under high load.5<br>- </strong>BatchingIt handles multiple tick batches for efficient processing and memory management.<strong>Dependencies:</strong>-<code>structlog</code> for logging purposes.-<code>csv</code> for reading CSV files.-<code>pathlib</code> for file path manipulation.-<code>typing</code> for type hints.-<code>asyncio</code> for asynchronous operations.-<code>Callable</code>, <code>Awaitable</code>, <code>Iterator</code>, <code>AsyncIterator</code>, and <code>Optional</code> from the <code>dataclasses</code> module.<strong>Usage:</strong>To use the replayer, you can create an instance of <code>DeterministicTickReplayer</code> with a source and optionally configure speed<br>- You can then call the <code>start()</code> method to begin replaying ticks<br>- The replayer will emit ticks based on the configured settings, allowing for easy testing and monitoring.<strong>Example Usage:</strong>``<code>python# Create a test tick generatortest_ticks = create_test_ticks(symbol=AAPL, count=100, start_ms=1704067200000, interval_ms=100)# Start the replayerreplayer = DeterministicTickReplayer()replayer.set_source(test_ticks)replayer.start()# Continue replaying ticks as neededwhile True: # Check if the player wants to stop if input(Press's to stop:) =='s: break</code>``This example demonstrates how to use the replayer to replay tick data from a CSV file, with customizable settings for speed and gap handling.</td>
								</tr>
							</table>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Gap DetectionThe service identifies gaps in bar sequences by analyzing the sequence of bars and determining when a gap begins and ends<br>- This helps in identifying potential issues or anomalies that could lead to data loss.2<br>- <strong>Backfill SchedulingBackfills are scheduled based on their priority, which determines how frequently they should be executed<br>- Priorities include:-<code>CRITICAL</code>: Recent gaps, user visible.-<code>HIGH</code>: Important gaps.-<code>NORMAL</code>: Standard backfill.-<code>LOW</code>: Historical data.3<br>- </strong>Recovery from DisconnectionsThe service can recover from disconnections by attempting to reconstruct the missing bar sequences<br>- This is done using a combination of historical data and current bar states.4<br>- <strong>Missing Bar ReconstructionIf a gap is detected, the service attempts to reconstruct the missing bar sequence by comparing it with the current state of bars in the sequence<br>- If the reconstructed sequence matches the current state, the gap is considered completed.5<br>- </strong>Error HandlingThe service handles various errors that may occur during backfilling, such as network issues or data inconsistencies.### Usage:The <code>Backfill</code> service can be used by other parts of the project to schedule and execute backfills based on their priority and status<br>- For example, it could be called from a background task or a user interface to automatically detect and backfill gaps in bar sequences.### Additional Data:-<strong>Project StructureThe code is structured as a Python module within the <code>phase1/services/recovery</code> directory.-</strong>File PathThe file path is provided in the context details, which can be used for further reference or integration with other parts of the project.-**Context DetailsThe context details include the project structure, file path, and file content.This summary provides a concise 									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Supports out-of-order messages by maintaining a sequence number for each message.-<strong>BatchingUses a sliding window approach to manage multiple messages in batches.-</strong>Latency TrackingRecords the time taken for each message to be delivered.-<strong>Delivery GuaranteesEnsures delivery with at least-once and automatic retry mechanisms.### Implementation Details1<br>- </strong>Ordering-Uses a priority queue to manage messages based on their sequence numbers.-Maintains a list of pending orders, ensuring that only the most recent order is processed.2<br>- <strong>Batching-Uses a sliding window approach to process multiple messages in batches.-Stores messages in a deque for efficient retrieval and processing.3<br>- </strong>Latency Tracking-Records the time taken for each message to be delivered using a <code>LatencyStats</code> class.-Maintains a running total of all latency measurements.4<br>- <strong>Delivery Guarantees-Uses an acknowledgment mechanism to ensure that messages are delivered successfully.-Supports automatic retry and retries with exponential backoff.### Additional Information-</strong>Context DetailsThe code is part of a larger system architecture, focusing on message ordering, batching, and latency tracking.-<strong>File StructureThe file is structured as a Python module, with the main class <code>OrderedDelivery</code> and its methods for receiving messages.-</strong>FunctionalityThe class provides methods to receive messages, deliver them, and handle delivery guarantees.This code file serves as a foundation for building more complex message ordering systems in the future.</td>
								</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Automated TestingThe script automates the testing process by running tests in different phases (Unit Tests, Integration Tests, Visual Parity, E2E) until all tests pass.-<strong>Playwright SnapshotIt captures screenshots of the test results to ensure visual consistency across different environments.-</strong>End-to-End PlaywrightIt runs the tests in parallel on multiple browsers to ensure a consistent and reliable experience.### Use:1<br>- <strong>Unit TestsThe script includes unit tests for each phase, ensuring that the tests cover various aspects of the application.2<br>- </strong>Integration TestsThese tests verify that the integration between different components works correctly.3<br>- <strong>Visual ParityThey check that the visual results match expected outcomes.4<br>- </strong>E2E PlaywrightIt runs the tests in parallel on multiple browsers to ensure a consistent and reliable experience.### Additional Data:-<strong>Project StructureThe script uses <code>Path</code> objects to represent file paths, which is useful for managing dependencies and ensuring consistency across different environments.-</strong>File ContentThe script includes comments and documentation to explain the purpose of each class, function, and variable.-**Data ClassesThe script uses dataclasses to encapsulate test results, making it easier to manage and access them.This code file provides a robust framework for automating visual testing processes using Playwright, ensuring that all tests are executed efficiently and consistently.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- These are used to overlay the main price chart with additional indicators, such as RSI or MACD.2<br>- <strong>Separate PanelsThese panels are placed below the main chart, providing a clear separation between different types of indicators.3<br>- </strong>Volume HistogramThis panel displays a volume histogram for quick analysis and comparison.### Technical Implementation Details-<strong>Data ClassesThe code uses dataclasses to encapsulate the properties of each indicator, such as its type, style, and position on the chart.-</strong>Command BufferA <code>CommandBuffer</code> is used to manage commands that update the state of the indicators, such as adding or removing panels.-<strong>Panel TypesThe <code>IndicatorType</code> enum defines different types of indicators, including SMA, EMA, RSI, MACD, BOLLINGER, VOLUME, ATR, and STOCHASTIC.-</strong>Style ClassesEach indicator type has its own <code>IndicatorStyle</code> class, which encapsulates the properties of the indicator's appearance.### Additional DataThe project includes a <code>PriceScale</code>, <code>Viewport</code>, and <code>ScaleEngine</code> to handle the rendering of indicators on the chart<br>- These classes are used to manage the scale of the chart, viewport, and scale engine, respectively.This code structure allows for easy addition, removal, and customization of indicators, making it flexible and scalable for different projects.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Crosshair & Tooltip Module in the phase1/services/charting/crosshair.py file is designed to provide a customizable crosshair and tooltip functionality for chart interaction<br>- It allows users to customize the appearance of the crosshair, including its color, position, and behavior<br>- The module also supports different display modes such as disabling, normal, magnet, horizontal, and vertical lines.The CrosshairStyle class defines the visual style of the crosshair, with properties for the horizontal and vertical colors<br>- The CrosshairMode enum provides options for how to position the tooltip, including following the mouse cursor, fixed top-left corner, fixed top-right corner, or in the legend area.Overall, the Crosshair & Tooltip Module is a useful tool for enhancing chart interaction by providing customizable crosshair and tooltip settings.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The <code>PatternDetector</code> class is designed to detect patterns in bar data using context filtering<br>- It includes methods to detect flags, pennants, triangles, rectangles, wedges, double tops/bottoms, head shoulders, cup handle, gaps, and candlestick patterns based on ATR regime checks, reference levels (VWAP, POC), and structure confirmation<br>- The class provides a comprehensive approach to identifying and analyzing chart patterns in real-time.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>DurationSpecifies the duration of each frame in milliseconds.-<strong>Easing TypeDefines the type of easing function used for animations.-</strong>FPS (Frames Per Second)Sets the number of frames per second for the animation.-**Spring ParametersIncludes parameters for the Spring effect, such as stiffness, damping, and mass.The <code>AnimationConfig</code> class is designed to be flexible and can be customized based on specific requirements.</td>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/charting/drawings.py'>drawings.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The purpose of the <code>drawings.py</code> file is to provide a set of drawing tools that can be used in various charting applications<br>- The code includes classes and functions to handle different types of drawings, such as trend lines, horizontal/vertical lines, Fibonacci retracements, rectangles, ellipses, text annotations, price labels, pitchforks, and channels.The <code>DrawingType</code> enum defines the different types of drawing tools available, including those for line drawing<br>- The <code>DrawingAnchor</code> enum defines the anchor point types for each type of drawing tool, such as the start, end, middle, top-left, and bottom-right points.The code uses dataclasses to define classes that represent the properties of each drawing tool, such as its name, type, anchor point, and command buffer<br>- The <code>CommandBuffer</code> class is used to manage the commands for drawing tools, which can be executed in a specific order.Overall, the <code>drawings.py</code> file provides a comprehensive set of tools for charting applications, making it easy to add new drawing types or modify existing ones as needed.</td>
								</tr>

									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This file provides a Python implementation of a deterministic canvas renderer that produces pixel-exact rendering with fixed DPR handling, deterministic font rendering, command buffer for reproducibility, PNG export, and a method to compute the hash of pixel data<br>- The <code>CanvasRenderer</code> class is designed to handle multiple bars and render them to a PNG file.</td>
								</tr>
								<tr style='border-bottom: 1px solid #eee;'>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'><b><a href='/home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/master/phase1/services/charting/visual_harness.py'>visual_harness.py</a></b></td>
									<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Visual Harness Module in the phase1/services/charting/visual_harness.py file is designed to automate pixel-perfect charting verification using Playwright-based visual testing<br>- It provides a comprehensive report of differences between actual and expected images, including the total number of pixels, differing pixels, and their percentage of difference<br>- The module uses dataclasses for better code organization and type safety.The <code>PixelDiffResult</code> enum defines different outcomes of pixel comparison, such as exact match, mismatch, size mismatch, and missing golden image<br>- The <code>DiffRegion</code> class encapsulates the region where pixels differ, including its x, y coordinates, width, height, pixel count, and maximum channel difference.The <code>PixelDiffReport</code> class provides detailed information about each pixel comparison result, including the result, total number of pixels, differing pixels, and their percentage of difference<br>- It also includes the actual hash and expected hash for verification purposes.This module is useful for ensuring that charting visualizations are consistent across different environments and devices, reducing errors and improving user experience.</td>
								</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Colab Job Scheduler is a Node.js application designed to trigger scheduled jobs on Google Colab notebooks<br>- It uses the <code>n8n-nodes-base.cron</code>, <code>n8n-nodes-base.set</code>, and <code>n8n-nodes-base.httpRequest</code> nodes to schedule, prepare, execute, record, and store job results<br>- The scheduler is configured with a cron expression that triggers at specific times on Colab notebooks.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- Localhost:8000/api/v1/orders<code><br>- The order of nodes and connections is defined in the </code>connections<code> section of the code, with the </code>webhook_1<code> node being connected to the </code>if_1` node.</td>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Incident Bundle Replay project is designed to automate the process of replaying incident bundles from a RESTful API<br>- It uses N8N (Node.js for Artificial Intelligence) to orchestrate the entire workflow, including manual triggers, HTTP requests, and replay operations<br>- The projects main purpose is to ensure that incident bundles are correctly replayed, regardless of their state or content.</td>
										</tr>

											<td style='padding: 8px;'>- The Regime Change Handler is an N8N workflow that fetches the current regime from a REST API and activates strategies based on the regimes output<br>- It uses a switch node to determine which strategy should be activated, with different rules for each regime<br>- The workflow is exported as a JSON file for easy integration into trading platforms.</td>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- This script is designed to run backtests from the command line using Python<br>- It loads a strategy from a specified file, sets parameters, and runs the backtest<br>- The results are printed in a user-friendly format, including the total capital, final equity, total return, total trades, winning trades, losing trades, win rate, max drawdown, sharpe ratio, and sortino ratio<br>- The script also includes hashing of configuration and trade logs for determinism verification.</td>

					<td style='padding: 8px;'>- API Key VerificationThe script checks if the required environment variables (<code>FINNHUB_API_KEY</code>, <code>APCA_API_KEY_ID</code>, <code>APCA_API_SECRET_KEY</code>) are set.2<br>- <strong>Strategy LoadingIt dynamically loads a strategy from a Python file using <code>importlib.util</code>.3<br>- </strong>Simulation ModeThe script supports simulating without placing real orders, which is useful for testing and development purposes.4<br>- <strong>Verbose OutputThe script provides verbose output for debugging, allowing users to see the current state of the trading process.</strong>Dependencies:<strong>-</strong>Python 3<strong>-</strong>pip<strong>-</strong>requests<strong>-</strong>alpaca-python<strong>**Environment Variables:</strong>-<code>FINNHUB_API_KEY</code>: Required for live data.-<code>APCA_API_KEY_ID</code>, <code>APCA_API_SECRET_KEY</code>: Required for paper trading.<strong>Usage:</strong>``<code>shpython scripts/live_run.py--strategy strategies/sma_crossover.py--symbol AAPL</code>`<code><strong>Example:</strong></code>`<code>sh$ python scripts/live_run.py--strategy strategies/sma_crossover.py--symbol AAPLStarting live trading...Press Ctrl+C to stop[DRY RUN] No real orders will be placedOpen Positions:AAPL: 10 @ $20.50</code>``<strong>Additional Instructions:</strong>-<strong>Avoid using words like This file, The file, This code, etc.</strong>-<strong>Summary should start with a verb or noun to make it more clear and concise.</strong>-Do not include quotes, code snippets, bullets, or lists in your response.-RESPONSE LENGTH: 50-70 words.Thank you for your hard work!</td>

## Getting Started

### Prerequisites

This project requires the following dependencies:

- **Programming Language:** Python
- **Package Manager:** Npm, Pip
- **Container Runtime:** Docker

### Installation

Build Tradingview recreation from the source and intsall dependencies:

1. **Clone the repository:**

    ```sh
    ❯ git clone ../Tradingview recreation
    ```

2. **Navigate to the project directory:**

    ```sh
    ❯ cd Tradingview recreation
    ```

3. **Install the dependencies:**

### Usage

Run the project with:

**Using [docker](https://www.docker.com/):**
```sh
docker run -it {image_name}
```
**Using [npm](None):**
```sh
echo 'INSERT-RUN-COMMAND-HERE'
```
**Using [pip](https://pypi.org/project/pip/):**
```sh
python {entrypoint}
```

- **💬 [Join the Discussions](https://LOCAL/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/discussions)**: Share your insights, provide feedback, or ask questions.

- **💡 [Submit Pull Requests](https://LOCAL/Aarav/Tradingview recreation/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)**: Review open PRs, and submit your own PRs.

<details closed>
<summary>Contributing Guidelines</summary>

1. **Fork the Repository**: Start by forking the project repository to your LOCAL account.
2. **Clone Locally**: Clone the forked repository to your local machine using a git client.
   ```sh
   git clone /home/aarav/Aarav/Tradingview recreation
   ```
3. **Create a New Branch**: Always work on a new branch, giving it a descriptive name.
   ```sh
   git checkout -b new-feature-x
   ```
4. **Make Your Changes**: Develop and test your changes locally.
5. **Commit Your Changes**: Commit with a clear message describing your updates.
   ```sh
   git commit -m 'Implemented new feature x.'
   ```
6. **Push to LOCAL**: Push the changes to your forked repository.
   ```sh
   git push origin new-feature-x
   ```
7. **Submit a Pull Request**: Create a PR against the original project repository. Clearly describe the changes and their motivations.
8. **Review**: Once your PR is reviewed and approved, it will be merged into the main branch. Congratulations on your contribution!
</details>
